This does not matter here because (a) the mounted `acl.conf` stays the single source of truth - the api and the shell tools only ever write it, and the reloader re-reads it on every reload; and (b) the `EMQX_AUTHORIZATION__SOURCES` env pin **resets the path back to the mounted file on the next broker restart** (also verified live). The shell backstop (`emqx_authz:update` with `path=/opt/emqx/etc/acl/acl.conf`) likewise resets the path and recompiles from the mounted file - the two mechanisms produce identical rules and never fight over content.
- **Non-fatal, coalesced, feature-flagged.** A reload failure only logs a warning - it NEVER fails an issuance or unclaim (the cron/deploy reload remains the backstop). Reloads run on a single background daemon thread and are coalesced over a short debounce window (default 300 ms), so a burst of grant changes collapses into one REST call that reads the latest file. The bean exists only when `voltpilot.enrollment.broker-authz-reload.enabled=true` AND an `api-url` is set, so dev, tests and non-enrollment deployments never need EMQX.
- **`reloadNow()` returns a boolean + `reloadNowBlocking(attempts, between)` for the STARTUP self-heal.** The async coalesced `requestReload()` (issuance/unclaim) stays fire-and-forget, but the startup ACL self-heal must KNOW whether EMQX actually re-read the healed file so it can log a loud ERROR if not (a healed file the broker never re-reads still leaves the device denied). So `reloadNow()` returns `true` only when EMQX accepted the push, and `reloadNowBlocking` retries a brief rolling-deploy blip (bounded, sleeps between attempts, never throws) and returns the final outcome; `EnrollmentService.selfHealBrokerAclOnStartup` calls it synchronously and ERROR-logs the break-glass on failure (see the ACL grant-ordering bullet in the secure-broker section).
- **Auth.** Either an EMQX API key (HTTP Basic, stateless - preferred) via `broker-authz-reload.api-key`/`api-secret`, OR the dashboard user/password (`username`/`password`, a cached login token, re-login on 401). The prod compose uses the latter, reusing `EMQX_DASHBOARD_USER`/`EMQX_DASHBOARD_PASSWORD` already shipped by the emqx service - no new secret. (Note: creating an API key via the EMQX REST `POST /api_key` is broken on 5.8.3 - `INTERNAL_ERROR` badmatch; use the dashboard UI or a bootstrap file to mint one, or just use the dashboard credentials.)
- **Config** (`voltpilot.enrollment.broker-authz-reload.*` / `VOLTPILOT_BROKER_AUTHZ_RELOAD_*`): `enabled` (default false; **prod compose sets true**), `api-url` (the REST base incl. `/api/v5`, prod `http://emqx:18083/api/v5` on the internal network - the dashboard port stays loopback-only to the host), `api-key`+`api-secret` OR `username`+`password`, `timeout` (PT5S), `debounce` (PT0.3S). **Prod `.env` additions:** none required beyond the existing `EMQX_DASHBOARD_PASSWORD` - the feature reuses it; `VOLTPILOT_BROKER_AUTHZ_RELOAD_ENABLED=false` opts out. **Needs a redeploy** to take effect (new api env). The deploy-workflow + cron `reload-broker-authz.sh` is deliberately KEPT as defense in depth.
- **Two-constructor footgun (@Autowired is load-bearing).** `BrokerAuthzReloader` has TWO constructors - the production `@Value`-injected one and a package-private test seam (`Path, FileSourceUpdater, long, ScheduledExecutorService`). The production constructor **must** stay annotated `@Autowired` so Spring selects it: with multiple constructors and none marked, Spring cannot pick the injection constructor, falls back to a (non-existent) no-arg constructor, and the whole api context crash-loops on startup with `No default constructor found` the moment `broker-authz-reload.enabled=true` matches the `@ConditionalOnProperty` (i.e. in prod). This shipped once (#42) because the unit/live tests construct the class directly and never exercised the Spring wiring with the flag on.
- **Tests.** `BrokerAuthzReloaderTest` (pure unit, always runs): reload pushes the current file content, is non-fatal when the broker call throws / the file is missing, and coalesces a burst into one call picking up the latest content (mocked `FileSourceUpdater`). `BrokerAuthzReloaderContextTest` (pure Spring slice via `ApplicationContextRunner`, always runs - the regression guard for the footgun above): boots the reloader with the flag ENABLED + minimal EMQX config and asserts the bean is created via its `@Value` constructor (and is absent when the flag is off); it fails with the exact `No default constructor found` crash if the `@Autowired` is dropped. `BrokerAuthzReloaderTest` also pins the boolean return (`reloadNow()` true/false) and `reloadNowBlocking` (retries-then-succeeds + gives-up-bounded-and-never-throws). `AclSelfHealBrokerE2eTest` (Testcontainers, `*Test`/`disabledWithoutDocker`, runs in the gate when Docker is present) is the **real-EMQX end-to-end proof of the whole self-heal chain**: a corrupted acl.conf denies a real MQTT SUBSCRIBE, then `AclGrantWriter.normalizeInPlace()` + `BrokerAuthzReloader.reloadNowBlocking()` (the two @PostConstruct steps) flip the SAME device to allowed on the running broker via the REST reload - proving file-normalize → EMQX re-read → allow, not just the file text. `BrokerAuthzReloaderLiveIT` is an **opt-in** live proof of the real HTTP path against a running EMQX (skipped unless `EMQX_REST_URL` is set; not collected by surefire's default `*Test` pattern) - used to verify the flip live. The existing `EnrollmentApiTest` exercises issuance/unclaim with the reloader UNCONFIGURED (default disabled -> `ObjectProvider` empty -> no-op), proving the enrollment path is unaffected when the feature is off.

## Standalone edge simulator (`tools/edge-simulator`)

An **installable-anywhere** simulated edge device: a single dependency-light Python program (only `paho-mqtt`) that publishes a believable PV/battery/load day to any VoltPilot broker, so a "device" on another LAN host writes into the captain's stack and shows up in the portal. It is **separate from** the Node-RED `edge/` (which models SunSpec/Modbus hardware) - do not conflate them. Full docs in `tools/edge-simulator/README.md`.

- **Contract-exact.** Publishes to `ems/{tenant_id}/{site_id}/{device_id}/telemetry` at QoS1 (+ `.../status` heartbeat), payload verbatim per `docs/contracts/mqtt-telemetry.schema.json` (`schema_version "1.0"`; note the field is **`ts`**, not `timestamp`). Feeds the existing ingest pipe unchanged. Identity defaults mirror the `demo` dev seed (`…0001/…0002/…0003`), so a no-arg local run lands in the demo telemetry view.
- **Zero-touch mode.** `--host <broker> --ref <edge-ref>` performs the provisioning handshake (see "Zero-touch device onboarding") instead of taking UUIDs: hello-retry until the ref is claimed in the portal, adopt the retained config, then publish normal telemetry. Explicit `--tenant-id/--site-id/--device-id` still work and skip the handshake.
- **Config** via CLI > env (`EDGE_SIM_*`, also honors `VP_TENANT_ID/SITE_ID/DEVICE_ID`) > optional `.env`. Plain `1883` and **mTLS `8883`** (device certs from `tools/pki/provision-device.sh`; TLS auto-enables on 8883 or when a client cert is set). No SNI-override flag - `init-ca --domain --ip` puts both in the server cert SAN so dialing either verifies; `--insecure` is the dev escape hatch.
- **Simulation.** Diurnal PV (zero at night, noon peak), household load profile, battery charge-midday/discharge-evening (SoC clamped 10-100%), `power_kw = load - pv + battery` (signed +import/-export), mild noise (seedable). `--time-scale N` replays a full day fast (e.g. 288 → a day in 5 min); `--time-scale 1` tracks the real clock. `--count N` exits after N messages.
- **Packaging.** `requirements.txt`, `Dockerfile` (multi-arch, non-root), example `voltpilot-edge-sim.service` systemd unit for a Pi, and an **opt-in compose service `edge-simulator`** under its **own `sim` profile** (NOT `edge`, so it never double-publishes with `edge-nodered`): `docker compose --profile sim up -d --build edge-simulator`.
- **Tests/proof.** `python3 -m pytest tools/edge-simulator/test_edge_sim.py -q` (or `python3 test_edge_sim.py`) runs fully offline - validates generated payloads against the **real** schema, the power-balance identity, PV day/night, battery cycle, SoC bounds, topic format, config precedence. Live proof: `tools/edge-simulator/proof/publish_and_verify.sh` publishes N and shows rows land in TimescaleDB (needs the `edge`-profile ingest path up). The real paho wire path (CONNECT + QoS1 PUBLISH/PUBACK) was verified against an in-process broker stub since this sandbox blocks Docker.
## VoltPilot Edge-App (`edge-app/`): the installable customer-side edge

The productized two-layer edge the captain decided on (2026-07-02); one docker-compose package the customer installs on their device.
Full docs in [`edge-app/README.md`](edge-app/README.md).
It is SEPARATE from `edge/` (the thin dev Node-RED edge in the cloud-stack compose) and from `tools/edge-simulator` (the MQTT-publisher sim) - do not conflate the three.

- **Layer 2 = `edge-app/core` (Go, module `.../edge-app/core`, binary `vp-edge-core`)** - identical at every customer, the ONLY thing talking to the cloud:
  first-boot HTTPS **enrollment** (EC P-256 key on-device -> CSR -> poll; consumes the api's enrollment endpoints exactly; conflict/unknown-ref states surfaced in German), the single outbound **mTLS MQTT** link (paho; broker derives identity from the cert CN),
  **honest failure states in the enroll state machine** (`enroll.State` - so `:8484` distinguishes "broken" from "waiting for you to claim", not one cheerful "warte" for everything): `portal_nicht_erreichbar` on a dial error / timeout / 5xx during CSR upload or cert poll (a 404 stays `pending`; clears itself when the portal answers again), `geraet_fehler` on a LOCAL first-boot failure (unwritable `/data`, key/CSR build - `enrollAndConnect` retries with capped backoff instead of silently exiting the goroutine), `cloud_fehler` when the cert is on disk but `startCloud` fails (retried with backoff, distinct from a blip), and `cloud_getrennt` when a live link drops (the pairing checklist moves OFF the green `verbunden` so it agrees with the Cloud stat), plus **`geraet_entfernt` for a CONFIRMED cloud-side removal (unclaim)**: the reconcile loop escalates a SUSTAINED, uninterrupted run of definitive clean-404 "not claimed" answers from a REACHABLE portal (`enroll.UnclaimDetector`, `VP_UNCLAIM_CONFIRM_MINUTES`/`_POLLS`, default 20 min + 4 consecutive polls; dial errors/timeouts/5xx RESET the run so an outage can never trip it, and a never-claimed device never reaches the loop) - on confirmation the cloud link is torn down, the store-and-forward buffer PAUSES (`Snapshot.BufferPaused`, "Aufzeichnung pausiert" in the UI - never unbounded growth toward a dead identity), identity/keys stay ON DISK, the onboarding gate re-opens the claim step with the ref, and continued polling makes a re-claim adopt the new identity and resume normally (a same-identity answer after a false alarm also self-heals). `getCertificate` also rejects a claim response with no `mqttHost`/`mqttPort`. The dashboard's `renderPairing` maps each to a visible error box + German guidance; the "alles Weitere passiert automatisch" hero copy shows only once an inverter is configured, and a `#inverterBanner` CTA shows until then (no inverter = the Node-RED read flow is idle = no telemetry). The buffer exposes a `DataLoss()` flag (set when eviction discards NEVER-published entries during an outage that overran the retention horizon, cleared once the backlog drains) -> `Snapshot.BufferDataLoss` -> the Cloud stat warns "älteste Messwerte werden verworfen" instead of the pending count silently plateauing.
  **while-connected identity re-reconciliation** (`enroll.Enroller.Reconcile` + `agent.reconcileLoop`, `VP_RECONCILE_INTERVAL_SECONDS`, default 300 s, also nudged on every cloud disconnect): the device re-polls `GET /enrollment/{ref}/certificate` and, if the portal now reports a DIFFERENT `device_id` (an unclaim+re-claim, or a `down -v`+re-claim, mints a new device row and the api re-issues the cert against the stored CSR - same key), **adopts** the new cert/identity on disk and tears down + rebuilds the cloud link on the new topics (reconnect is mandatory - the broker enforces the cert-CN identity via its ACL; the store-and-forward buffer is stamped at publish time so pending entries flow to the new id). Without this a device that enrolls only at first boot would keep publishing under its stale persisted `device_id` forever, and the portal's Geräte tab (per-device liveness keys on `telemetry.device_id = device.id`) would read **"wartet auf erste Daten"** permanently while the site-keyed Übersicht still showed values (device-identity-drift bug). Layer-2 follow-up (NOT done, deliberately): preserving the device id across re-claim (deterministic `UUIDv5(external_ref)` or soft-delete unclaim) would remove the drift trigger, but a deterministic id makes the re-claim cert-poll hit the "unchanged id" branch that skips the ACL-grant rewrite (unclaim's `onDeviceUnclaimed` removed it) -> the cert loses broker access in prod unless claim also re-writes the grant; that expands scope into the security path, so Layer 1 (which fully fixes the symptom) ships alone.
  **store-and-forward** telemetry (segmented JSONL disk ring, default 48 h, cursor + monotonic seq persisted, ack-after-QoS1-confirm, replay oldest-first with ORIGINAL timestamps - safe because ingest is idempotent per (device, time)),
  **schedule cache + execution** (frozen `mqtt-schedule` contract; plan persisted to disk for reboot-without-network; 20-min staleness window mirrored from the contract's `x-failsafe`, ALSO bounded by plan AGE - a retained-schedule redelivery on reconnect whose `generated_at` is older than the window + 5-min slack is stale immediately, so a dead optimizer's plan can't look fresh again (plan.Parse anchors ReceivedAt to generation time; 2026-07-12 audit fix); guards clamp EVERY setpoint: rated power band, SoC bounds, observed §14a envelope on import AND export, and - when the plan carries `grid_charge_allowed=false` (EEG site, P5) OR omits the field entirely (fail-safe since the 2026-07-12 audit fix; only an explicit `true` releases it) - charge ≤ MEASURED PV production (`guards.Limits.SolarOnlyCharge`; PV-bus semantics since FK3, captain decision 2026-07-16 - the house may import its load in parallel; unknown pv then blocks charge entirely - a compliance guard never charges blind; the plan-carried posture also ANDs into the `edge/setpoint` `grid_charge_allowed` adapter gate, most-restrictive-wins with `VP_GRID_CHARGE_ALLOWED`); stale/no plan -> self-consumption fallback = PV − load, which never grid-charges by construction),
  an **embedded local MQTT bus** (mochi-mqtt in-process, topics `edge/telemetry`, `edge/setpoint` (retained), `edge/status`), and the German read-only **local energy dashboard** on `:8484` (see the dashboard bullet below; `/health` for watchdogs). Config: env > `config.json` > defaults (`config.Defaults()`); an unset ref is generated once and persisted.
- **Local energy dashboard (`core/internal/web` + `core/internal/history`, served on `:8484`).** The customer-facing view is a professional live energy monitor, not just a pairing page: at-a-glance KPI cards (PV / Batterie-SoC + laden/entladen / Hausverbrauch / Netzbezug↔Einspeisung), an animated SVG **energy-flow** diagram (direction encodes charge/discharge + import/export), and hand-rolled `<canvas>` **live time-series charts** (Leistung PV/Last/Netz/Batterie with a 15 Min/1 Std/3 Std toggle + a SoC chart; `static/charts.js`, HiDPI-aware, hover tooltip). The pairing/onboarding card is a **guided, enforced two-step sequence** (see the enforced-onboarding-gate bullet below) that stays prominent until *Verbunden*, then collapses. **Backend:** the agent keeps an in-memory ring of recent `edge/telemetry` samples (`internal/history.Ring`; the battery line is the **MEASURED** `battery_power_kw` register - or a physical 0 on a provably batteryless family - never the balance derivation, per the 2026-07-17 house-consumption standard; `Sample.BatteryKw()` derived stays as an internal cross-check the agent drift-logs against the register; absent measurements stay absent/null - never coerced to 0); `web.Handler(state, inverterCtrl, history)` serves `GET /api/history?minutes=N` (initial series + `server_now_ms` for device-clock alignment) and streams live updates over **SSE** `GET /api/stream` (`event: state` every 1 s + `event: sample` per new point; client falls back to `/api/state` polling when SSE is unavailable). All assets are bundled locally (NO CDN - the edge may have no UI internet); static files are `//go:embed`-ed, so **rebuild the `core` binary after any `static/*` change** (the running binary serves the OLD embedded assets otherwise). The inverter-selection surface (`inverter.js` + `inverter.css`, now an AREA of `einrichten.html`) shares `dashboard.css`/`tokens.css`/`shell.css` and layers its form + **model-picker** styles in `inverter.css`; the old `app.css` is retired. Since the 2026-07-27 two-page rebuild the pairing/onboarding card is gone from the dashboard - see "Edge `:8484` web app: TWO pages" below. The model picker is a custom searchable listbox (combobox+listbox ARIA: type-to-filter with `<mark>` highlighting, arrow/Enter/Escape keys, family group headers straight from the catalog's `brand.families`, explicit selected state + footer summary, empty state; brands with few models skip the search row) - still fully catalog-driven, the form POSTs `{brand, model, connection}` unchanged. `TestInverterPageServesModelPickerStructure` pins the embedded page structure/assets. Read-only: nothing here controls the inverter. Tests: `internal/history` (measured-battery wire shape, JSON omit-missing, ring evict/window) + `internal/web` (state envelope carries the device clock, `/api/history` measured battery, `/api/stream` pushes state then new samples). NOTE the cloud PORTAL still renders battery derived from the balance (rollups/`HistoryRepository`) - correct while the edge publishes `load_kw` = the standard house (derived == measured then), but surfacing the measured register cloud-side needs an ADDITIVE telemetry field (candidate follow-up, deliberately not done here).
- **Local Fahrplan view (`:8484` "Batterie-Fahrplan" card, read-only).** The device holds the full validated dispatch plan cached on disk (`plan.Store`); the dashboard now renders it instead of only the one-line mode status. **Data path:** `web.PlanController` (the agent's `CurrentPlan()`) serves `GET /api/plan` = `plan.(*Plan).BuildView(now)` (slots, freshness against the 20-min `plan.StaleAfter` window, `active_index` = the executing slot only while fresh, `curtailed` flag) PLUS the live guard-clamped `setpoint_kw`/`mode`/`slot_start` from state so the section is self-contained; `has_plan:false` with no `plan` key = the honest empty state ("Noch kein Fahrplan empfangen"). No SSE plan event: `static/plan.js` (`window.VPPlan.onState`, called from `dashboard.js applyState`) refetches `/api/plan` whenever the streamed state's `plan_received` changes, and ticks the "Jetzt" line + active-slot highlight + freshness every second client-side. The chart is a dependency-free `<canvas>` bar chart (charge up/green `--batt`, discharge down/blue `--brand-deep`, active-slot band, curtailment ticks in `--grid-c`, hover tooltip) matching `charts.js`. **`plan.go` now parses the OPTIONAL `pv_limit_kw` per slot** (`Slot.PvLimitKw`, dropped if negative/NaN) - display only, execution (`ActiveSetpoint`/`applySetpoint`) is unchanged. **Scope limit worth knowing:** the frozen schedule contract sent to the edge (`mqtt-schedule.schema.json`, `additionalProperties:false`) carries only `battery_setpoint_kw` + `pv_limit_kw` per slot - NO per-slot grid power - so the "cyan when the slot grid-charges" distinction is NOT derivable on-device (the portal derives it from the `schedule` hypertable's `grid_kw` + `pv_kw`, which the edge never receives); the curtailment (`pv_limit_kw`) marker IS shown, and grid-charge tinting would need a contract+optimizer change (out of edge-app scope). Remember: `plan.js`/`static/*` is `//go:embed`-ed - rebuild the core binary after editing. Tests: `plan_test.go` (pv_limit parse incl. drop-negative, `BuildView` active/curtailed/stale) + `internal/web` (`/api/plan` present/absent, Fahrplan page structure `TestFahrplanSectionServed`).
- **Enforced two-step onboarding gate (`:8484`, not-yet-claimed devices only).** The customer MUST connect the inverter FIRST, then claim the device in the portal - and the wrong path is not reachable. The gate is derived **server-side** in `web.go`'s `envelope()` (`deriveOnboarding`): `onboarding_step` is `"inverter"` (connect the inverter), `"claim"` (inverter delivers data -> claim in the portal), or `"done"` (device already paired - a certificate is on disk, `paired()` matches `verbunden`/`zertifikat_erhalten`/`cloud_getrennt`/`cloud_fehler`). The GATE for unlocking the claim step is `inverter_connected` = **an inverter is configured (`Snapshot.Inverter.Configured`) AND at least one telemetry reading arrived (`!LastTelemetry.IsZero()`)** - configured-but-silent stays locked. **Belt-and-suspenders enforcement:** while `claim_unlocked` is false the `ref` is BLANKED in the `/api/state` envelope, so the reference cannot leak into the UI before the inverter is proven to work (this only blanks the UI copy; the device's own enrollment reads its ref from config, never from this envelope, so the background key/CSR/cert handshake is untouched). Frontend (SINCE the 2026-07-27 two-page rebuild): the guided flow lives on `einrichten.html` (`commissioning.js` derives the four steps, `einrichten.js` renders them), the reference is revealed only once `claim_unlocked` is true, and the whole block recedes to one green line when all four steps are done. `dashboard.js` no longer renders any onboarding - the Betrieb page only SHOWS the resulting state via the status hero, plus the `#inverterBanner` link to Einrichten while no inverter is configured. Gate logic proven by `web` tests `TestOnboardingGateHoldsClaimUntilInverterDeliversData` (a→b→c: no-inverter / configured-no-data / delivering) + `TestOnboardingGateDoneOncePaired`. Remember: `static/*` is `//go:embed`-ed - rebuild the `core` binary after editing.
- **Layer 1 = `edge-app/nodered`** - the per-customer I/O layer, **wired by VoltPilot, never by the customer** (editor LAN-only behind adminAuth, `VP_NODERED_PASSWORD` - FAIL CLOSED since the 2026-07-12 audit fix: settings.js refuses to start when the password is unset or the old default `voltpilot`, mirroring install.sh; compose no longer defaults it). Ships the **vp-palette** (`@voltpilot/node-red-vp-palette`: `vp-core` config node + `vp-telemetrie`/`vp-sollwert`/`vp-status`, preconfigured to `core:1883`) and three template tabs: **"SunSpec (Simulator)"** (ENABLED - hardware-free e2e against `edge-sim`), **"SunSpec Wechselrichter (Vorlage)"** (DISABLED - the per-customer starting point; enable + point at the real inverter + disable the sim tab) and **"Deye (Vorlage)"** (DISABLED - see the Deye section below). The local-bus contract for wiring a **custom inverter** (the `edge/telemetry` flat measurement payload - `power_kw`/`soc_pct`/`pv_power_kw`/`load_kw`/`grid_limit_kw`/optional `ts`, all optional, kW + percent, QoS1/no-retain - plus `edge/setpoint`/`edge/status`, the cloud-mapping, and the copy-paste function/`mqtt out` recipe) is the operator guide [`edge-app/nodered/CUSTOM-INVERTER.md`](edge-app/nodered/CUSTOM-INVERTER.md), linked from `edge-app/README.md`.

- **Deye transport = pick a `communication` method: `solarman_v5` (RECOMMENDED) or `at_cli` (fallback).** The Deye WiFi datalogger (LSW3 "stick") is read the ROBUST way via the **Solarman V5 protocol** = Modbus-RTU wrapped in a Solarman-V5 frame over **TCP 8899** (what Home-Assistant/pysolarmanv5 use), NOT the flaky AT-command UDP-48899 tunnel (dongle replies from a different source port / as broadcast get dropped by conntrack behind NAT). Both methods share the SAME register maps + decode - only the transport differs (`communication: 'solarman_v5'|'at_cli'` in the config; solarman_v5 default). The V5 frame codec (frame build/parse, logger-serial LE32 embedding, V5 byte-sum checksum, embedded Modbus-RTU fn-0x03 + its own CRC16, TCP-segment reassembly) is the canonical, **offline-unit-tested** **[`edge-app/nodered/deye/solarman-v5.js`](edge-app/nodered/deye/solarman-v5.js)** (`solarman-v5.test.js`, cross-checked against independent CRC/checksum impls incl. checksum/short-frame/exception cases); the flow's *"Solarman-V5 lesen"* function node carries a **synced copy** and owns the socket (needs `functionGlobalContext.net` in `settings.js` - wired). **CRITICAL: `serial` is the DATALOGGER serial (e.g. from the AP SSID `AP_<serial>` or the logger status page), NOT the inverter serial** - the #1 config mistake. Because the VoltPilot crew cannot reach a customer's logger, the operator runs the standalone dependency-free **[`edge-app/nodered/deye/solarman-probe.js`](edge-app/nodered/deye/solarman-probe.js)** ON THE EDGE VM (`node solarman-probe.js --ip <logger> --serial <n> --family <f>`; also `--start/--count` for a raw register dump) to prove the transport + confirm register maps against real hardware. Confirmed real logger for the on-hardware test: IP `192.168.0.28`, datalogger serial `2985159064`, port 8899, LSW3 stick on a **`SUN-*-SG01HP3-EU` 3-phase HV HYBRID** (~50 kW class; inverter serial `2407224048`, read from identity regs `0x0003..0x0007` as ASCII) - **NOT a string inverter** (an early guess the register dump disproved: regs `0x0050..0x00CC` are the hybrid's battery/charge CONFIG area - charge/discharge current `0x006C/0x006D`, capacity `0x0066`, charge voltages `0x0083/0x0086` - so the live measurements sit at `0x024C+`, decoded by the **`hybrid_3p`** family; the old `--family string` map read those config regs as measurements and produced 30 MW garbage). **`hybrid_3p` covers BOTH 3-phase hybrid lines** (SG04LP3 LV 2-MPPT **and** SG01HP3 HV 3-4-MPPT - same ha-solarman high-map): `soc 0x024C`, `batt 0x024E`, **PV = sum of all 4 MPPTs `0x02A0..0x02A3`**, **grid = the EXTERNAL CT total 32-bit `0x026B`(low)+`0x02C4`(high)** (deye_p3.yaml "External Power" = the connection-point clamp; the former `0x0271`/`0x02B2` "Grid Power" is a CONFIG-DEPENDENT alias - live-falsified 2026-07-17 on the captain's SUN-30K: alias read −23,7 kW = exactly the own PV while the true PCC export was 54,2 kW, the Deye's own load −30,5 = 23,7 − 54,2 proving it internally uses the external CT; the alias stays as decode FALLBACK for old narrower reads), **load = 32-bit `0x028D`(low)+`0x0293`(high)** - two read blocks: the device-identity register `0x0000` (LV/HV scale class) + the measurement block `024C0079` (0x024C..0x02C4, 121 regs). Addresses authoritative from **davidrapan/ha-solarman `deye_p3.yaml`** (+ Deye Modbus manual for PV3/PV4). **HV/LV power scale is AUTO-DETECTED, mirroring ha-solarman:** `deye_p3.yaml` gives PV Power + Battery Power a dual `scale:[1,10]` (LV=1 W, HV=10 W/decawatt) and Grid/Load NO scale (always watts); ha-solarman picks LV vs HV from the "Device" register `0x0000` (`const.py` `AUTODETECTION_DEYE`: LV `0x0005`/`0x0500`→`mod 0`→×1; HV `0x0006`/`0x0007`/`0x0600`/`0x0008`/`0x0601`→`mod 1`→×10, and `0x0008`/`0x0601` = "HV 3-Phase Inverter 20-50kw" = the exact SUN-30K-SG01HP3-EU class). `deye-decode.js` reads `0x0000`, applies the class to **PV+battery only** (never grid/load/SoC). `power_scale` is now a MANUAL OVERRIDE/fallback (an explicit `1`/`10` wins; falls back to `1` when `0x0000` is unreadable - never fabricates a class); the catalog default is `0` = auto. **Correction of an earlier overclaim:** #49 confirmed the *family selection* (`hybrid_3p` over the wrong `string` map), NOT `scale=1` on the captain's SG01HP3 - so an SG01HP3 (HV) is now correctly ×10 by auto-detect. The 32-bit grid/load also fixes a `power_kw` wrap past ±32.7 kW on a 30-50 kW unit. VERIFY-on-device stays: the **grid sign** (`invert_grid_sign`). Proven by `deye-decode.test.js` (LV→×1, HV incl. 0x0008/0x0601→×10, grid/load always ×1, 32-bit grid/load within+beyond ±32.7 kW, manual override + unreadable-register fallback) + `flows-sync.test.js`. **The `at_cli` fallback** uses the **bundled `deye` CLI** (`github.com/s10l/deye-logger-at-cmd`, a Go binary; the nodered Dockerfile builds it in a `golang` stage - default branch is **`main`** (pinned via `DEYE_REF`), main package is in `src/`, binary lands at `/usr/local/bin/deye`; offline fallback = drop a release binary there). The operator sets `flow.deye_wechselrichter = [{ip, communication, port, serial, mb_slave_id, family, invert_grid_sign, invert_batt_sign, power_scale, limit_stages}]` in the *Deye-Konfiguration* node; `family` (`string`/`hybrid_1p`/`hybrid_3p`/`micro`) selects the register map. **The four families reconcile ha-solarman's FIVE Deye definitions** (`deye_string`→`string`; `deye_2mppt`+`deye_4mppt`→`micro`; `deye_hybrid`→`hybrid_1p` low map; `deye_sg04lp3`→`hybrid_3p` high map) - see the model→family table in `DEYE.md`. **`string` and `micro` report ONLY generation** = the inverter's own AC-output register (`0x0050`/`0x0051` for string, `0x0056`/`0x0057` for micro; 32-bit low-word-first ×0.1→W), which is post-inverter and already sums ALL MPPT strings (MPPT-count-agnostic, 1-4). A string/micro inverter has NO grid-meter or house-load register (that is a hybrid feature), so the pre-#49 `string` map's grid/load fields (`0x00C6`/`0x00CB`) were fictional and were removed; `micro` (formerly write-only) now reads AC output too. The `at_cli` exec nodes shell out to `deye -t ip:48899 -xmb <REGHEX4><COUNTHEX4>` (read, fn 0x03, answer `+ok=0103<byteCount><data><crc>`); **32-bit values are LOW-WORD-FIRST** `(reg[+1]<<16)+reg[0]`. Reads are a **sequential** per-family block loop (never two `deye` processes at once on the one logger): `hybrid_1p` one 22-reg block; `hybrid_3p` TWO blocks (the 1-reg device-identity register `0x0000` for the LV/HV scale class + the 121-reg measurement block `024C0079`); string/micro a 2-reg AC-output pair - all well under the 125-reg fn-0x03 max. Only the canonical cloud fields are published to `vp-telemetrie` (soc only for hybrids); **battery power is NOT a cloud telemetry field** (it rides the LOCAL bus as `battery_power_kw` = the measured battery term of the house-consumption standard + the dashboard battery line; the cloud still derives battery from the power balance), and `grid_limit_kw`/§14a is not exposed by these Deye registers. **Sign convention is firmware-dependent** (sunsynk inverts vs ha-solarman raw): the `invert_grid_sign`/`invert_batt_sign` flags + the on-device calibration procedure (midday PV surplus → grid negative; forced charge → batt positive) are the answer - never trust a library's sign blindly; **signs stay VERIFY-on-device** (the hybrid_3p HV/LV scale is now auto-detected from `0x0000`, see above). **Family auto-detect:** the "Familie erkennen" inject probes `-xmb 00B80001` (low-map SoC) vs `-xmb 024C0001` (high-map SoC) - exactly one reads a sane 0..100 → hybrid_1p vs hybrid_3p (string/micro have no SoC, chosen manually). **String/micro power-limit write** (active-power curtailment 0..100%) goes to reg `0x0028` via `-xmbw 00280001 02 <pct>`, with optional `limit_stages` snapping - this is the clearly-labeled EMS control stop where VoltPilot maps its §14a/curve onto device stages. **Hybrid battery charge/discharge control is deliberately OUT OF SCOPE** (no direct watt setpoint; indirect via System Work Mode + Time-of-Use + amp current limits, model-dependent, can damage a battery) - left as a documented TODO with researched control registers in `DEYE.md`. The canonical, unit-tested decode (parser + register maps + scaling + power-limit/auto-detect helpers) lives in **[`edge-app/nodered/deye/deye-decode.js`](edge-app/nodered/deye/deye-decode.js)** (`node --test edge-app/nodered/deye/`, no hardware/network); the flow's function nodes carry a **synced copy** of that logic (a Node-RED flow is self-contained JSON and cannot `require` a repo file). **SoC drop-don't-fabricate gate (real prod symptom - 12k LV SoC time series spiking 0/100):** a Solarman logger that can't reach the inverter (typical at night) still returns a well-framed, CRC-valid response - usually an all-zero register block or, on a misaligned frame, an out-of-range value. Decoding those verbatim published `soc_pct=0` (axis floor) or `soc_pct>100` (chart 0-100 axis CLIPS it to the ceiling → the "100" spikes), while the true stepped curve stayed faintly underneath; the register map itself (`0x024C`) is CORRECT per ha-solarman - it is NOT a wrong-register bug, and NOT the family auto-detect (the self-wiring path routes on the configured `family`, never re-probes). Fix: `decode()` DROPS a battery-family reading whose SoC is unreadable or outside `(0,100]` (returns null → NO sample published, never a fabricated 0; `socPlausible()`), so the chart gaps instead. `flows-sync.test.js` now guards the inline `auto-deye-decode` copy against the module (incl. the drop cases). **The gate is enforced on EVERY live display path, not only the Deye decode** (real prod symptom #2 - the `:8484` SoC tile rendered a raw read as "1.270 %"): the edge core drops an implausible-SoC sample at its ONE local-bus ingest choke point (`agent.onLocalTelemetry` via `guards.SocPlausible`, the Go twin of `socPlausible()`; `TestSocPlausible` pins the same vectors as `deye-decode.test.js` so the two cannot drift - the refCheckChar/EdgeRef precedent), so garbage from ANY Layer-1 flow (generic Modbus, custom wiring, a pre-gate Deye build) can never reach the dashboard tiles/energy flow (state snapshot), the live charts (history ring), the cloud (telemetry buffer + status-heartbeat `lastRawSoc`) or the setpoint guards; every display keeps its last good value and the existing >90 s freshness UI (`.stale` + "keine aktuellen Daten") turns honest on its own because `LastTelemetry` only advances on kept samples (`telemetry_gate_test.go`). **Transient IN-BAND spike gate (`guards.Despiker`, real prod symptom #3 - the captain's SoC momentarily reading e.g. 2 % between two steady 94 % samples, "genauso bei anderen Werten", and the same on other channels):** the `SocPlausible` gate above only rejects OUT-OF-band values, so a physically-impossible but in-band single-sample jump passes it. `guards.Despiker` runs right after `SocPlausible` at the SAME `agent.onLocalTelemetry` choke point and gates **every recorded channel, per channel** (`soc_pct`/`power_kw`/`pv_power_kw`/`load_kw`/`grid_limit_kw`; a rejected channel is handled in THAT sample only, the good channels in the same sample still flow). **Hold-last output (captain's amendment - continuous line, no gaps):** a rejected value is NOT dropped-to-absent, it is REPLACED in place with that channel's last accepted value, so every recorded series (dashboard tiles/energy flow, live-chart ring, cloud buffer, status heartbeat, setpoint guards) stays an unbroken line; there is still NO smoothing/interpolation - the substitute is exactly the last good reading held flat until a real value resumes or a genuinely new level is confirmed. **One unified rate model, per channel:** a reading is suspect when `|Δ| > margin + maxRatePerSec*elapsed` (scales with the real inter-sample gap). SoC is integrative → a tight `%-Punkte/s` bound; power channels CAN step fast (cloud edge, load switching) → a generous `kW/s` bound so real dynamics pass immediately and only larger, reverting excursions are caught; `grid_limit_kw` is now gated too but loosely (a real §14a step is small and passes on the margin, only gross garbage is caught). A gap beyond `despikeGapReset` (5 min) accepts a fresh baseline (a reboot never false-rejects). **Operator-configurable on the device (`guards.DespikeSettings` / `internal/guards/settings.go`):** "ab wann ein Wert als sprunghaft gilt" is set on `:8484` → **Einstellungen** (`static/einstellungen.{html,js,css}`, linked from the dashboard + inverter topbars, its own card - NOT cluttering the energy monitor): a global sensitivity preset **Aus / Locker / Normal / Streng** picks safe per-channel numbers, and an "Expertenwerte" section overrides the raw `max_rate_per_sec` + `margin` per channel (a custom set is tagged `benutzerdefiniert`; each channel can also be individually disabled). The **default is Normal**; its SoC numbers reproduce the shipped 1.0 %/s + 5 % (behavior-compatible), and the power channels get conservative in-band defaults (Normal 15 kW/s + 15 kW) chosen so real household/PV dynamics are NEVER eaten - the operator tightens to Streng (5 kW/s + 5 kW) if in-band spikes persist. Settings persist in `data_dir/despike.json` (atomic write, mirrors `inverter.json`) and apply LIVE via `Despiker.Reconfigure` (no restart; the running per-channel state carries across, so continuity is not broken). Served/edited by `GET`/`POST /api/despike` (`web.DespikeController`, the agent implements it; validation rejects negative/NaN/absurd/all-zero-while-enabled with a German message → 400). **Accept-after-confirmation (no deadlock, no lookahead):** a suspect jump is held-last but remembered as a candidate; after `despikeConfirmCount` (3) consecutive agreeing readings the new level is adopted, so a genuinely resynced BMS, a real §14a envelope change or a real fast power step larger than the envelope converges instead of holding the stale level forever (the earlier readings stay held - drop-never-alter). Rejections are counted in the state snapshot total (`DespikedDropped`) AND per channel (`Despiker.DroppedByChannel`, surfaced in `/api/despike` and shown as "N gefiltert" on each channel card, polled live) and logged at debug rate-limited (`despikeLogInterval` 30 s). The SoC `SocPlausible` OUT-OF-band gate above still drops-to-absent (the whole read is untrustworthy) - hold-last is scoped to the despiker's in-band spikes. Proven by `guards/despike_test.go` (94→2→94 SoC symptom held-last, steady discharge passes, persistent level converges, long-gap reboot, power spike-and-return held, genuine fast power step passes at Normal, **Streng catches what Normal lets through**, disabled channel passes, grid_limit legit-step-passes-but-gross-garbage-caught, per-channel counters, live Reconfigure) + `guards/settings_test.go` (presets complete/distinct, default=Normal+shipped SoC, Normalize known-preset/custom/re-tag/validation, store round-trip) + `agent/despike_gate_test.go` (end to end: the 2 never surfaces as a value, the series stays continuous, a glitchy multi-channel sample keeps its good channels, **live SetDespike tightening catches a spike on the ingest path**) + `web/web_test.go` (GET/POST/validation, settings-page structure + //go:embed guard). **Physical-envelope gate (`guards.Envelope`, real prod symptom #4 - the captain's `:8484` Verlauf still showing single-sample ~26 kW spikes on the DERIVED battery curve after he set "Streng"):** the rate model above allows `margin + rate*elapsed`, so at a real ~10 s cadence even "Streng" tolerated tens of kW in one step (old Streng power `5 kW/s + 5 kW` → `55 kW` allowed at 10 s), and because the dashboard battery line is DERIVED (`battery = grid - load + pv`, `history.Sample.BatteryKw`), an unbalanced grid spike that passed the rate gate snapped the green line and back. Two fixes: (1) **"Streng" power channels were tightened** to `1.0 kW/s + 5 kW` (→ `15 kW` at 10 s) so a ~26 kW single-sample step is caught by the rate gate alone; Aus/Locker stay permissive. (2) **A preset-INDEPENDENT physical envelope** derived from the CONFIGURED inverter model (`inverter.Model.RatedKw`, added to the catalog; `Catalog.RatedKw` + `inverter.FamilyHasBattery`): a 12 kW inverter can neither generate nor move ~26 kW whatever the preset says. `guards.EnvelopeFor(ratedKw, hasBattery)` bounds **PV** (`rated*2 + 2` kW, generous for DC over-sizing) and, for battery families, **battery charge/discharge** (`rated*1.5 + 2` kW). Crucially **pure grid import is NOT inverter-bounded** (it follows the house connection), so grid is caught INDIRECTLY: the envelope enforces the battery bound on the DERIVED battery and, when the power balance yields a beyond-envelope battery, holds the offending MEASURED channel (largest jump vs its last in-envelope value) - so an UNBALANCED grid spike (grid jumps, load/pv steady → derived battery out of envelope) is held, while a genuine high grid draw (balanced by load → derived battery ~0) passes. The envelope runs AFTER the despiker at the SAME `agent.onLocalTelemetry` choke point with its OWN last-good state (hold-last, continuous line), is a HARD bound (never adopted-after-confirmation - a persistent beyond-envelope reading is a scaling/model misconfig to fix via `power_scale`/model, and the offender-by-jump heuristic only fires on an actual jump `> envMinJumpKw`, so a steady beyond-envelope state is left visible not fabricated-held), and is INACTIVE when no inverter is selected or the model has no rating (`generic_modbus`/SunSpec). Rebuilt on `SetInverter` and at boot (`agent.applyEnvelope`); its per-channel drop counters fold into the same `/api/despike` "N gefiltert" surface (`GetDespike`) and the `DespikedDropped` total. Proven by `guards/envelope_test.go` (captain's grid spike caught at any preset + held-continuous, balanced high grid passes, direct PV bound, PV-spike-over-grid offender ID, no-battery family derives nothing, persistent-not-adopted, live model reconfigure), `guards/despike_test.go` (Streng catches the 26 kW@10 s step, Aus passes it), `agent/envelope_gate_test.go` (12 kW model: the spike never reaches the derived battery even with the rate gate OFF; inactive-without-inverter passes it) and `inverter/inverter_test.go` (`RatedKw`/`FamilyHasBattery`). **Follow-up option for OLD edge builds (NOT built - the captain's device until he updates still uploads spikes):** the server-side `timescale-writer` could add a STATELESS belt-and-braces heuristic (e.g. drop an out-of-`(0,100]` SoC on insert, matching the portal-side `sanitizeSoc`), but a stateful per-device rate/jump gate does NOT belong in the writer (it must stay a stateless idempotent inserter); the portal-side render gap for a bad DB row is the existing mitigation. Belt-and-braces in the portal: the shared `sanitizeSoc` (`src/plausible.ts`, the single portal-side source) maps an out-of-range `socPct` to null in `TelemetryChart.tsx` (`connectNulls:false` → gap, never a clip-spike) AND backs the Übersicht "Batterie-Ladestand" tile, which falls back to the newest plausible reading with a calm "Stand vor X" note when that lags the live data by >5 min (the deviceLiveStatus window) - so a pre-existing bad DB row can't render anywhere either. Operator guide: **[`edge-app/nodered/DEYE.md`](edge-app/nodered/DEYE.md)** (per-family register tables, the auto-detect probe, the CLI install, config shape, sign calibration, and the honest scope note).
- **Inverter selection (`core/internal/inverter` + the local web app).** The customer/operator picks their inverter ONCE in the Edge-App web UI (`:8484` → **Einrichten** → Wechselrichter, `static/einrichten.html`+`inverter.js`): **brand → family/type → connection params**. The **communication method is fixed per brand** (the captain's rule, so the customer never picks a transport): `deye` → `solarman_v5` (WiFi datalogger, TCP 8899), everything else (`generic_modbus`) → `modbus_tcp` (TCP 502). The catalog (brands/families/**connection fields**) lives in `DefaultCatalog()` and is served by `GET /api/inverter`, so the form is fully data-driven and new brands/fields are additive - NO front-end change. The core `Normalize`s the request against the catalog (communication + label DERIVED server-side, defaults filled, per-transport validation: solarman_v5 REQUIRES the **datalogger** serial), persists it to `data_dir/inverter.json` (generated-once-persisted like the plan cache, survives restart), stamps it onto `state.Snapshot.Inverter` (so the dashboard shows the model), and publishes it **retained** on the local bus topic **`edge/inverter/config`** at boot AND on every change. The Node-RED tranche consumes that retained message to self-wire the right read adapter (no Admin API) - see the **Node-RED self-wiring** bullet below. Contract: **[`edge-app/INVERTER-CONFIG.md`](edge-app/INVERTER-CONFIG.md)** (topic/payload/per-communication connection fields/HTTP API). `web.Handler(state, InverterController)` gained the controller (the agent implements it) + `GET`/`POST /api/inverter` (400 with a German message on a validation failure). Read/monitoring selection only - NOTHING here controls the inverter. Tests: `internal/inverter` (Normalize defaults/derivation/rejections, BusPayload per-transport shape, store round-trip), `internal/web` (API round-trip + 400s via a fake controller), `internal/agent/inverter_test.go` (selection persists across restart; retained publish observed on the bus at boot and on change via a real localbus + paho subscriber). **Per-MODEL selection (captain's rule: every Deye model pickable individually, NO family grouping):** the Deye brand exposes a complete per-model list (`Brand.Models`, `deyeModels()` in `inverter.go`: SG04LP3 LV incl. the 12K, SG01HP3 HV, SG03LP1 1-phase, G03/G04 string, SUN*G3 micro), each mapping to its register-map `Family`; the UI (`einrichten.html`/`inverter.js`, searchable model dropdown) POSTs `{brand, model, connection}` and the core DERIVES the family. `edge/inverter/config` is **additive**: it now carries `model` AND `family`, `family` STAYS the Node-RED routing key (`inverter-routing.js`/flows.json unchanged, `schema_version` stays `"1.0"`), so a 12k LV can never be read with an HV profile. A bare `family` is still accepted (backward-compat). `Families` metadata stays as the register-map reference. **Fronius (Solar API, HTTP/JSON - like Home Assistant, NOT Modbus):** brand `fronius` → `communication: fronius_solar_api`; the edge does ONE HTTP(S) GET to the local Solar API **v1** `GetPowerFlowRealtimeData.fcgi` and maps `Site.P_PV`/`P_Grid`/`P_Load` + `Inverters["1"].SOC` onto the canonical channels in `edge-app/nodered/fronius/solar-api.js` (the read-only decode twin of `deye/solarman-v5.js`; the flow's HTTP reader/decoder function nodes carry a synced copy, pinned by `flows-sync.test.js`; `settings.js` exposes `http`/`https` like the Deye `net` precedent). ONE generic model (the API is self-describing, no per-model register map) and NO `RatedKw` (so the physical-envelope guard stays inactive). **Signs are VERIFY-on-device** (grid: no inversion - Fronius `P_Grid` already matches +import/-export; `load_kw = -P_Load`; missing `SOC`/`P_Akku` → field omitted, never a fabricated 0 - Deye's socPlausible discipline). `insecure_tls` handles the GEN24 self-signed-cert HTTPS redirect firmware; scope is **v1 only** (a v0 legacy Datamanager is an idle-safe non-goal). Read-only by construction (NOT in `CERTIFIED_CONTROL_FAMILIES`), like Deye. Operator guide: **[`edge-app/nodered/FRONIUS.md`](edge-app/nodered/FRONIUS.md)**. The `fronius_solar_api` SOURCE variant (a Fronius as an additional Erzeuger/Netz source, not the primary inverter) is **now wired** in the "Energiequellen (automatisch)" tab (E6, `readFroniusReading` in `build-flows.js`, `sources.busEntry` forwards `insecure_tls`/`invert_grid_sign`), proven by `sources-read.e2e.test.js` against an in-process Solar API server. **Fronius over SunSpec Modbus TCP (the read path for a device whose Solar API does NOT work, e.g. a Fronius Eco 27.0-3-S):** a SEPARATE catalog brand `fronius_sunspec` (`communication: fronius_sunspec`, port 502) - kept a distinct brand so the per-brand-fixed-communication rule stays intact ("Fronius" = Solar API, "Fronius (Modbus / SunSpec)" = SunSpec Modbus). It runs a REAL SunSpec model-discovery walk (`nodered/sunspec/model-discovery.js`, already existed for the planned-only control side) plus the NEW measurement decode + socket reader `nodered/sunspec/sunspec-live.js` (`sunspec-live.test.js`): decode the inverter measurement model (float 111/112/113 via IEEE-754 `W`, or int+SF 101/102/103 via `W`×10^`W_SF`) → `pv_power_kw = max(0, W)/1000` (a string inverter's AC output IS its PV), surfacing `St`(incl. `THROTTLED`)/`Evt1`. Addresses are DISCOVERED live (only field offsets within a model are fixed); a real 0 W (night) is kept, unreadable → absent (never a fabricated 0). `family`=`sunspec_live`, `connection` carries `unit_id`(first-class, default 1)+`model_type`(auto-detected)+`invert_grid_sign`. Wired into the self-wiring auto tab as a 5th router branch/reader node (embeds the two modules, `flows-sync.test.js` drift guard + `sunspec-live.e2e.test.js` drives it against an in-process Modbus server) and into `test-read.js` ("Verbindung testen"). The Eco Model carries `RatedKw=27` so the physical-envelope guard engages. Scope (Increment 1): ONE `(ip, unit_id)` read - NO multi-inverter aggregation (the two-Eco split is a later increment), NO control (curtailment stays planned-only). A meter (model 21X) decoder exists but is minimal/optional and NOT wired live (the Eco site has no meter). **VERIFY-on-device (captain follow-up on the real Eco):** proven only against fixtures + an in-process Modbus server here. Go catalog: brand/comm/`FamSunSpecLive` + `froniusSunspec*()` in `inverter.go`.
- **Node-RED self-wiring from the inverter selection (`edge-app/nodered`, the capstone - "vorne auswählen, hinten ist alles verdrahtet").** Layer 1 reads the retained `edge/inverter/config` and runs the matching read adapter with **NO per-customer flow edit**. The always-on flow tab **"Wechselrichter (automatisch)"** in `flows.json`: a new **`vp-inverter-config`** vp-palette node subscribes the retained selection on `core:1883` and emits it (parsed/validated, drops malformed); a store function keeps it in `flow.inverter_config`; a poll timer drives a **"Router / Leseplan"** function that branches on `communication` → **`solarman_v5`** (Deye reader over TCP 8899 with the `family` register map) or **`modbus_tcp`** (generic Modbus-TCP reader FC3 over 502 with the `profile`); both decode to the canonical flat reading published via `vp-telemetrie`. No/unknown selection → **idle-safe** (node status only, publishes NOTHING - deliberately does not emit `edge/status`, so it never fights the Simulator tab's link state in pure-sim dev). **Source-of-truth + offline-tested modules** (the flow function nodes carry synced COPIES, since a Node-RED flow is self-contained JSON and can't `require` a repo file): `nodered/inverter-routing.js` (`parseConfig`/`route`), `nodered/modbus-tcp.js` (Modbus-TCP codec + the `sunspec` PROFILE = the compact `edge/sim` FC3 0..8 block), reusing `nodered/deye/{solarman-v5,deye-decode}.js` verbatim for the Deye path. `flows.json` is generated by **`nodered/build-flows.js`** (assembles the readable function bodies + keeps the Simulator tab/config nodes verbatim - re-run it and validate rather than hand-editing the escaped JSON). The former manual **"Deye (Vorlage)"** and **"SunSpec Wechselrichter (Vorlage)"** tabs are **retired** (the selection drives it now); the **Simulator tab stays** (enabled, independent, hardware-free e2e incl. the dev setpoint-write loop the compose e2e needs). The self-wiring path is **read-only** - the string/micro power-limit write, the Deye family auto-detect (use `deye/solarman-probe.js` on the edge VM instead) and hybrid battery control are NOT in it. Tests (`node --test nodered/*.test.js nodered/deye/*.test.js`, offline): `inverter-routing.test.js`, `modbus-tcp.test.js`, `flows-sync.test.js` (runs the INLINED flow function bodies in a vm and asserts they match the modules - the drift guard), `modbus-tcp.e2e.test.js` (drives the real flow reader against an in-process Modbus-TCP server), plus `vp-palette` `nodes_spec.js` (the `vp-inverter-config` node against an aedes bus, incl. retain-on-connect). **vm-realm gotcha:** objects a function-node body builds have the vm context's own `Object`/`Array` prototypes, so `deepStrictEqual` against outer-realm values fails - normalize via a JSON round-trip (or spread arrays) before comparing.
- **Node-RED template auto-reseed on image update (`edge-app/nodered/reseed-entrypoint.sh`, real footgun fix).** The `nodered` compose service mounts the named volume `vp-nodered-data:/data`, which SHADOWS the image's `/data` after the first run - so a pulled image's new `flows.json`/`settings.js`/`vp-palette`/`node_modules` never reached the running Node-RED, which kept executing the STALE flows from the volume (a Deye decode fix in `:latest` was silently ignored). Fix: the Dockerfile bakes the template at the NON-shadowed path **`/opt/vp-template`** (+ a content-hash `.vp-template-version` marker over the whole tree) and wraps the base image's `./entrypoint.sh` with a re-seed entrypoint that, on every start, copies the template into `/data` ONLY when the marker differs (`cp -RP` = recursive + preserve the `vp-palette` symlink, NOT ownership - portable across BusyBox/GNU/BSD; files `cp -f`), then `exec`s the base entrypoint (which still forwards SIGTERM). Idempotent: an up-to-date volume start is a SILENT no-op; an update re-seeds and logs loudly what it replaced. The flows are VoltPilot-owned template + self-wiring (the customer never hand-edits them; `flow.inverter_config` re-populates from the retained MQTT selection), so re-seeding from the image is CORRECT, not data loss - genuine Node-RED runtime state in `/data` (`.config.*.json`, credentials, context) is left untouched, and `vp-edge-data` (core identity/certs) is never touched. So `docker compose pull && up -d` now auto-applies flow updates with no volume wipe. **The re-seed MUST run as ROOT (prod-down 2026-07-09, PR fm/vp-reseed-perm-fix).** An OLDER image seeded vp-palette/node_modules into `/data` at BUILD time (`COPY ... /data`), so on a pre-existing `vp-nodered-data` volume those dirs are ROOT-owned; PR 117 ran the entrypoint unprivileged (`USER node-red` before it), so `rm -rf /data/vp-palette` hit `Permission denied` (rm needs write on the root-owned parent), `set -e` aborted, the container looped, and the edge went dark - no telemetry. The host-only test never caught it (its `/data` was owned by the test user). Fix: the Dockerfile drops the `USER node-red` (entrypoint starts as root) + `apk add --no-cache su-exec` (base is **Alpine/BusyBox** - no gosu, and BusyBox `setpriv` can't `--reuid`, so su-exec is the tool); the entrypoint re-seeds as root, `chown -R node-red:node-red "$DATA_DIR"` (gated on `DID_RESEED` so a healthy restart skips the recursive walk), then `exec su-exec node-red:node-red "$@"` - so Node-RED still runs UNPRIVILEGED, only the re-seed gains root. Deploying the fixed image SELF-HEALS an already-bricked device (no manual chown). Non-root starts (host tests / `--user`) skip the drop and behave as before. Tests: `edge-app/nodered/reseed.test.sh` (Docker-free: stale-volume replace + runtime-state-kept + silent no-op + fresh seed + content-hash marker + a structural root/su-exec/chown guard) and `edge-app/nodered/reseed-perms.docker.test.sh` (Docker: builds a minimal image on the real Alpine base, seeds a ROOT-owned volume, proves the root entrypoint recovers it AND that the old unprivileged path bricks; skips cleanly without Docker). Operator note in `edge-app/DEPLOY.md` "Betrieb"/"Update".
- **Sim mode is first-class:** `docker compose --profile sim up -d --build` in `edge-app/` also starts the SunSpec Modbus simulator (built from `../edge/sim`) = the FULL edge app end-to-end with zero hardware.
- **Host-networking mode for LAN UDP loggers (`docker-compose.hostnet.yml`).** Many Deye/Solarman WiFi dongles (UDP 48899) don't answer a bridged container: the dongle's reply arrives from a different source port / as broadcast and conntrack drops it after SNAT, so the `deye` read returns empty. The fix is the standard one for Solarman/USR loggers - put Node-RED directly on the host LAN (no NAT). Opt-in **override** (never a profile - `network_mode: host` is incompatible with profiles/port maps): `docker compose -f docker-compose.yml -f docker-compose.hostnet.yml up -d`. It sets `network_mode: host` on `nodered`, drops its port maps (`ports: !reset []`), and points the local bus at the core's host-loopback (`VP_CORE_HOST=127.0.0.1`, `VP_CORE_PORT=${VP_BUS_PORT:-1884}` -> the `127.0.0.1:1884:1883` mapping the `core` service already publishes). **Load-bearing:** `vp-core.js` reads `VP_CORE_HOST`/`VP_CORE_PORT` with **precedence over** the seeded flow config (`process.env.X || config.x || default`), so the bus redirect needs no flow edit. Caveats: the Node-RED editor lands on fixed host port `1880` (no `VP_NODERED_PORT` mapping in host mode); the SunSpec `edge-sim` service name is unreachable from the host net (hostnet is for the real LAN inverter, not the simulator); `network_mode: host` is a Linux-only feature (no effect on Docker Desktop macOS/Windows). Default bridged + `--profile sim` paths are unchanged (override is opt-in). Docs: DEPLOY.md "LAN-Logger nicht aus dem Container erreichbar?" + `nodered/DEYE.md` section 6. Regression test: `vp-palette` `VP_CORE_HOST/PORT override the flow config`.
- **Standalone guided installer `edge-app/install.sh` (recommended path for a NEW device; `install.sh` is the ONLY file the device needs).** A POSIX-bash, `shellcheck`-clean, idempotent installer that **generates its own `docker-compose.yml`** (no repo clone required) and formalizes the `DEPLOY.md` flow: prerequisite checks (docker / `docker compose` v2 / daemon), registry login guidance (`git.tecmaxx.de`), `.env` creation seeded from `.env.example` (production endpoints as defaults per captain decision: `VP_PORTAL_BASE_URL=https://portal.voltpilot.de`, `VP_MQTT_HOST=mqtt.voltpilot.de:8883`), `pull` + `up -d` in **real mode only** (`core` + `nodered` + the OTA-Stufe-3 apply sidecar `updater` since 06.08.2026, always **with `--profile ota`** so a fresh box observes+reports from the start - the per-device autonomy switch stays OFF by default, unchanged; NEVER `--profile sim`), prominent Reference-ID + portal-claim guidance, and PASS/FAIL verification. Details: `edge-app/AGENTS.md` "Teil C".
  - **Generated compose = images-only, REAL mode (captain decision 2026-07-08 "fully standalone").** `generate_compose()` emits the `core` + `nodered` services pulled from `git.tecmaxx.de` with `pull_policy: always`, mirroring the repo `edge-app/docker-compose.yml` real-mode services EXACTLY (image names, env var names + `:-defaults`, volume names `vp-edge-data`/`vp-nodered-data`, port mappings, incl. the empty `VP_DEV_*` passthroughs) but with **NO `build:` contexts** (a device never builds) and **NO `sim`/`edge-sim` profile**. The output is byte-identical to the repo compose's `docker compose config` (default, no-sim) minus the `build:` lines, so a pulled stack behaves like a repo `up -d`. **Lockstep:** if the repo compose's real-mode image refs/env/volumes/ports change, `generate_compose()` must be updated too - `edge-app/test/install-selfcheck.sh` guards this (compares `install.sh --print-compose` against the repo `docker compose config` and fails on drift; docker-free structural checks + shellcheck always run, the equivalence check runs when docker is present).
  - **Writes into the CURRENT working directory** (`TARGET_DIR="$(pwd -P)"`, no longer resolves/`cd`s into a repo `edge-app/`); `dc()` pins `--project-directory "$TARGET_DIR" -f "$COMPOSE_FILE"` so it is CWD-robust and always loads the target dir's `.env`.
  - **Compose idempotency + no-clobber:** a compose file WE generated carries a first-line marker (`# @voltpilot-edge-install: ...`); on re-run our own file is refreshed in place (byte-identical, data volumes untouched), a **foreign/hand-edited** file is NEVER silently overwritten (interactive keep/overwrite prompt; `--non-interactive` keeps it; `--force-compose` or `--reconfigure` overwrites). `--reconfigure` regenerates BOTH `.env` and compose; `--force-compose` only the compose; `--print-compose` emits the generated compose to stdout and exits (writes/checks nothing - the docker-free self-check + an ops escape hatch).
  - **Safety by design (unchanged):** REFUSES the default `voltpilot` Node-RED password (loops until a non-default is set; non-interactive dies), NEVER sets the `VP_DEV_*` hatches (only warns if a kept `.env` carries them), never `down -v`/wipes `vp-edge-data`/`vp-nodered-data`, no secrets echoed, `.env` written 0600. Flags `--help`/`--non-interactive` (env-driven, needs `VP_NODERED_PASSWORD` non-default)/`--reconfigure`/`--force-compose`/`--print-compose`/`--skip-pull`/`--dry-run` (dry-run validates the GENERATED compose via a throwaway file, writes nothing).
  - **The machine-readable device signal it reads is the core's ungated `GET http://127.0.0.1:<VP_WEB_PORT>/health`** (`edge-app/core/internal/web/web.go`): `{ref, pairing_state, cloud_connected, uptime_seconds}` - unlike `/api/state`, `/health` returns `ref` even before the claim gate, so it is the right endpoint for a headless installer. Healthy pre-claim `pairing_state` is `warte_auf_beanspruchung` (enrollment reached the portal, CSR uploaded, polling); `portal_nicht_erreichbar`/`geraet_fehler`/`schluessel_konflikt`/`referenz_unbekannt` are the actionable failure states (constants in `core/internal/enroll/enroll.go`). `cloud_connected=true` only AFTER the operator claims in the portal, so the installer verifies "reached the portal + both containers running" as PASS and offers an optional 5-min wait for the cloud link rather than falsely claiming success. Recommended in `edge-app/DEPLOY.md` + `README.md`; the manual steps stay documented as the fallback. Not runnable headless end-to-end (needs a real device + claim) - verified here via shellcheck, `--dry-run`, `--print-compose` + `docker compose config` equivalence to the repo compose, `.env` generation, idempotency/foreign-file protection, safety refusals, and a docker-shim harness.
- **Standalone updater `edge-app/update.sh` (recommended update path for a RUNNING device).** One command in the deploy dir: refreshes the compose file(s) to current AND pulls + `up -d --remove-orphans` + verifies via `/health` (PASS/FAIL with pairing/cloud state + rollback hints). It **SOURCES install.sh** for `generate_compose()`/the marker/the helpers (install.sh's `main` is guarded behind a `BASH_SOURCE` check) - ONE compose template, so it needs install.sh next to it (fails with a fetch hint otherwise). Deploy-model aware: installer-marker compose -> regenerated from the template; git-tracked compose (repo clone) -> `git pull --ff-only` (never force/stash/reset); hand-edited -> kept unless `--force-compose`. A used `docker-compose.hostnet.yml` is auto-detected via the running nodered container's `network_mode` (`--hostnet` forces it when containers are stopped) and refreshed from a template embedded in update.sh that must stay byte-identical to the repo file (drift-guarded). HARD safety: never `down -v`/volume wipe, `.env` never rewritten (perms kept 600), `VP_DEV_*` never set. `--dry-run` degrades gracefully without docker (file-only checks). Self-check: `edge-app/test/update-selfcheck.sh` (docker-free core: template equality install==update, hostnet lockstep, safety greps, dry-run behaviorals; docker-gated merged-config validity); details in `edge-app/AGENTS.md`.
- **Published images are MULTI-ARCH (`linux/amd64` + `linux/arm64`).** Raspberry Pi (arm64/v8) is the primary edge target, so `.forgejo/workflows/edge-images.yaml` builds a manifest list per tag (`:latest`, `:${sha}`) for BOTH `edge-app-core` and `edge-app-nodered` - `docker pull` picks the right variant per device automatically. Achieved on the amd64 runner via `docker/setup-qemu-action@v3` + `docker/setup-buildx-action@v3` and `platforms: linux/amd64,linux/arm64`; both Dockerfiles CROSS-compile their Go stages (`FROM --platform=$BUILDPLATFORM golang ... ARG TARGETOS TARGETARCH ... GOOS/GOARCH` - core's `vp-edge-core` and nodered's diagnostic `deye` CLI), so arm64 builds without emulation (CGO already disabled in both). The registry buildcache is shared across platforms. An amd64-only publish (the old workflow) caused `no matching manifest for linux/arm64/v8` on a Pi.
- **Dev escape hatches** (never on customer devices): `VP_DEV_TENANT_ID/SITE_ID/DEVICE_ID` (skips enrollment) + `VP_DEV_CLOUD_URL` (plain-MQTT cloud) - this is what the isolated compose e2e uses.
- **Tests:** `(cd edge-app/core && go test ./...)` - unit (guards clamp incl. §14a both directions, 20-min staleness -> fallback, buffer replay/eviction/restart, enrollment state machine incl. 409/422/key-conflict, identity **reconcile** no-op/adopt/pending, config precedence) + in-process **integration tests** (real mTLS mochi cloud broker + enrollment stub with a real CA: enroll -> telemetry with original ts -> retained schedule -> clamped setpoint on the local bus -> outage -> buffer grows -> reconnect -> ordered replay; and a re-claim mid-connection -> the edge adopts the new `device_id` and resumes publishing under it). `(cd edge-app/nodered/vp-palette && npm install && npm test)` - shaping units + node-red-node-test-helper against an in-process bus (aedes), incl. the `vp-inverter-config` node. `node --test edge-app/nodered/*.test.js edge-app/nodered/deye/*.test.js` - the self-wiring routing/codecs + the flow-vs-module drift guard + the live-socket generic-Modbus read (see the Node-RED self-wiring bullet). `edge-app/test/e2e-compose.sh` - isolated compose bring-up (own project name + high ports, stand-in mosquitto cloud; covers the `sim` profile): contract telemetry at the broker, retained schedule -> `edge-sim` logs the setpoint write, web `/api/state` shows `fahrplan` + inverter link up.
- **MVP non-goals** (documented in `edge-app/README.md`): OTA/auto-update, on-edge ML, local optimization beyond the self-consumption fallback.

## Production deployment (single VM/VPS, "deploy like saalo")

MVP-on-one-server: an external reverse proxy on another host terminates TLS (the company's **Nginx Proxy Manager** for the internal-VM deployment; Caddy stays documented as the alternative), the whole server-side stack runs from **`docker-compose.prod.yml`** (a self-contained, standalone file - NOT merged with the dev `docker-compose.yml`), and Forgejo does push-to-deploy once CI secrets are set. Full guide in [`docs/deploy.md`](docs/deploy.md); the recipe mirrors the saalo repo's proven pattern.

- **Two deploy paths, one compose.** (a) **Manual first deploy, no CI** (the leading `docs/deploy.md` section "Erstes Deployment (interne Ubuntu-VM)"): git clone to `/srv/docker/voltpilot` on the VM (the clone IS the deploy dir - the compose bind-mounts `infra/prod/`, `infra/mqtt/`, `deploy/keycloak/themes/` by relative path), fill `.env`, stage the MQTT certs (required - EMQX file-mounts them), then `docker compose -f docker-compose.prod.yml build && ... up -d`. Every app service carries a `build:` context next to its `image:` name, so `build` tags local images under the registry names and `up` uses them without pulling. **Seit dem Cutover VM -> k3s (03.08.2026) ist dieser Pfad HISTORISCH/Hand-Referenz** - die VM-Zwillinge sind gestoppt und der laufende Betrieb kommt aus dem Cluster. (b) **CI** (`.forgejo/workflows/deploy.yaml`): Images bauen/pushen + gitops-Tag-Bump; **der frühere SSH/SCP-Roll-out auf die VM ist ENTFERNT** (ein Lauf hätte die gestoppten Compose-Zwillinge wiederbelebt = zweiter Optimierer gegen dieselben Live-Anlagen; PR `fm/vp-deployfast-retire`).
- **Two public ports only.** `frontend` on `APP_PORT` (plain HTTP, firewalled so only the reverse-proxy host reaches it) and EMQX `8883` (mTLS, for devices - **TCP, not proxied by NPM's HTTP proxy hosts**; devices dial `<VM-IP>:8883` directly, or an NPM Stream). Everything else (api/keycloak/keycloak-db/timescaledb/redpanda/ingest/writer/collectors) is internal on the `voltpilot-prod` network; `1883`/`18083` are loopback-only. **The frontend's nginx is the single web entry point** (`frontend/portal/nginx.conf`): serves the SPA and reverse-proxies `/api/ -> api:8090` and `/auth/ -> keycloak:8080`, so the API and Keycloak stay internal. The proxy must forward `https://${DOMAIN} -> http://VM:${APP_PORT}` preserving `Host` and setting `X-Forwarded-Proto: https` - NPM's default proxy-host template does both (enable Block Common Exploits + Websockets Support); the `/auth` login flow breaks without that header.
- **Keycloak in prod mode.** `start --import-realm` against a **dedicated** `keycloak-db` (isolated from TimescaleDB, mirrors saalo). Served under the `/auth` relative path (`KC_HTTP_RELATIVE_PATH`, `KC_HOSTNAME=https://${DOMAIN}/auth`, `KC_PROXY_HEADERS=xforwarded`). Prod realm `infra/prod/keycloak/voltpilot-realm.json` parametrizes the browser origin, confidential secret and Portal-Admin password via `${VP_PUBLIC_ORIGIN}` / `${VP_API_CLIENT_SECRET:change-me}` / `${VP_PORTAL_ADMIN_PASSWORD:admin}` (Keycloak-26 realm-import placeholder replacement reads env vars as `${VAR}`/`${VAR:default}`; the Wildfly-era `${env.VAR}` prefix is NOT substituted and aborts the import - verified on 26.0.5; all vars set on the keycloak service). So the api's issuer is `https://${DOMAIN}/auth/realms/voltpilot` while JWKS is fetched internally at `http://keycloak:8080/auth/realms/voltpilot/...certs` (the same issuer-trap split as dev). The prod realm mirrors the dev realm's **admin increment** (platform-admin role, seeded `admin` Portal-Admin user, `realm-management` service-account grants, declarative user profile for `tenant_id`), and the prod api sets the matching env: `ADMIN_DB_PASSWORD` (the BYPASSRLS `voltpilot_admin` role would otherwise run on its hardcoded dev password) and `KEYCLOAK_ADMIN_BASE_URL=http://keycloak:8080/auth` (note the `/auth` path - the dev default without it 404s in prod). **Keycloak-26 prod-mode footgun:** `start --import-realm` strict-parses the realm JSON and ABORTS boot on unknown fields (e.g. a `_comment` key) - `start-dev` never caught this; docs for the prod realm live in `infra/prod/keycloak/README.md` instead.
- **VoltPilot Keycloak login theme** in `deploy/keycloak/themes/voltpilot/` (login only; saalo's structure: `parent=keycloak.v2` + one override stylesheet, so ALL flows incl. error/forgot-password pages inherit the styling). Colors/typography come from the portal design-system tokens (`frontend/portal/designsystem/tokens/*.css`), the wordmark is the real logo PNG copied from `designsystem/assets/`, DE/EN message overrides brand the titles, and both realm imports set `loginTheme: voltpilot` + enable `de` as the default locale. The theme dir is bind-mounted read-only into keycloak in BOTH composes; the deploy workflows scp it and `docker compose up -d --force-recreate keycloak` after `up -d`: in `start` mode themes are cached at boot AND pre-gzipped into `/opt/keycloak/data/tmp/kc-gzip-cache` on the container FS, which SURVIVES a plain `restart` - browsers (gzip) then still get the OLD css while curl (no gzip) sees the new one (haunted-cache footgun, verified on 26.0.5). Dev `start-dev` keeps the caches off, but an already-running dev keycloak needs ONE `up -d` recreate to pick up the new theme mount + realm setting.
- **Project Keycloak image (`deploy/keycloak/Dockerfile`) - the k8s-cutover prerequisite.** Compose bind-mounts the realm import + the login theme into the STOCK upstream image; Kubernetes cannot supply either the same way, so the GitOps Deployment pulls a project image that **bakes both in** and pre-runs `kc.sh build` (hence its `start --optimized`). Three facts that keep it correct: (1) the **build context is the REPO ROOT** (like `services/optimization`), because realm (`infra/prod/keycloak/`) and theme (`deploy/keycloak/themes/`) live in different directories - the CI matrix entry therefore carries an explicit `dockerfile:`; (2) exactly three Keycloak **BUILD** options are baked (`KC_DB`/`KC_HEALTH_ENABLED`/`KC_HTTP_RELATIVE_PATH`) and they MUST equal what the runtime supplies, or a `start --optimized` refuses to boot - everything host-/secret-specific stays runtime, so **no secret is in the image**; (3) the version pin lives in BOTH `ARG KEYCLOAK_VERSION` and the compose `image:` default and must move together (two Keycloak versions against one DB otherwise). All three, plus "does CI actually build it", are guarded by section 4 of `tools/deploy/verify-migration-deploy.sh` (proven to bite on each drift). Compose is switchable but UNCHANGED by default: `image: ${KEYCLOAK_IMAGE:-quay.io/keycloak/keycloak:26.0.5}` - with the var unset the resolved prod compose is byte-identical to before, and setting it to the project image leaves the mounts overlaying byte-identical files. `KEYCLOAK_IMAGE` is a full reference and is NOT pinned by `${IMAGE_TAG}`. Rationale + operator steps: [`deploy/keycloak/README.md`](deploy/keycloak/README.md); the `keycloak` service deliberately has **no compose `build:` block** (it would tag the local build under the upstream image name and shadow it).
- **Images for the CI path.** CI builds and pushes **nine** images to `git.tecmaxx.de/mamotec/voltpilot-ems/<component>:{latest,<sha>}` (`api`, `ingest`, `timescale-writer`, `frontend`, `market-data`, `forecast`, `optimization`, `flowc`, `keycloak`); the compose deploy pulls the first eight pinned to `${IMAGE_TAG}` = the commit SHA, while `keycloak` is built for the cluster and is opt-in on compose (see the bullet above). The build matrices of `deploy.yaml` and `deploy-fast.yaml` are kept in **lockstep** - add a component to both, or a gated deploy publishes a set the fast path does not. Each has a prod Dockerfile; the **frontend** Dockerfile grew an nginx.conf + `VITE_*` build args (`VITE_API_BASE` empty => same-origin `/api`; `VITE_KEYCLOAK_URL=https://${DOMAIN}/auth`) - the compose `build:` block passes the same args from `.env` for the manual path (changing `DOMAIN` means rebuilding the frontend).
- **Collectors** (all in the dev stack's `serve` mode, no hand-rolled loops): `market-data` (`serve --persist`, 6h refresh into `day_ahead_prices` over the superuser conn; source per `MARKET_DATA_SOURCE` - keyless energy-charts default, `entsoe` + `ENTSOE_SECURITY_TOKEN` switches to ENTSO-E, see `docs/deploy.md`), `weather-collector` (Open-Meteo `serve --persist`, 3h refresh into `weather_forecast`; reuses the forecast image with the `weather_collect` entrypoint) and `forecast` (the shadow-mode forecast collector `forecast_collect serve` - see "Shadow-mode forecasting"). The two non-RLS hypertables (`day_ahead_prices`/`forecast`) are created by the prod init script `infra/prod/timescale/00-collector-tables.sql` (mirrors the service migrations; runs on a fresh volume before the api's Flyway owns the core schema). Edge/Node-RED + the SunSpec sim are NOT in prod (they run on-device).
- **CI/CD** in `.forgejo/workflows/`: `deploy.yaml` (gated: test matrix over all services -> build+push all images -> `gitops-tag-bump`) and `deploy-fast.yaml` (dasselbe ohne Test-Gate). **Kein Job fasst die Prod-VM mehr an** (Cutover 03.08.2026): der `deploy`-Job samt scp/ssh-Action ist entfernt, `DEPLOY_HOST/USER/PASSWORD` werden nicht mehr gelesen; Argo CD deployt aus dem gitops-Repo. Benötigte Forgejo-Secrets: `FORGEJO_USERNAME/PASSWORD`, `DOMAIN`, optional `GITOPS_PUSH_TOKEN`. `edge-images.yaml` ist davon unberührt (Edge-Images sind kein VM-Deploy). No browser smoke suite yet, so that gate is deferred (the Testcontainers tests already cover ingest/RLS/OIDC).
- **CI-Zielpfad für den Cluster: der Deploy IST ein Commit (`gitops-tag-bump`, Captain-Go 2026-08-01).** Nach grünen Image-Builds schreibt derselbe Lauf den Bild-Stand ins **gitops**-Repo (`git.tecmaxx.de/mamotec/gitops`) fort: der Job setzt den `images:`-Block von `apps/voltpilot/overlays/prod/kustomization.yaml` auf `${GITHUB_SHA}` und committet als `ci(images): voltpilot-ems@<sha>`. Bewusst OHNE `:latest` und ohne Argo-CD-Image-Updater (Begründung im gitops-README „CI/CD-Zielpfad"); Rollback = `git revert`. Der Job hängt an `needs: build`, **nicht** am VM-Deploy - beide sind unabhängige Abnehmer derselben Images, der VM-Job bleibt bis zum Cutover unverändert. Argo steht auf manuellem Sync, der Bump landet also nur im Git. Vier Dinge, die man wissen muss: (1) **kein `kustomize edit set image`** - v5.8.1 (die von der gitops-CI gepinnte Version) formatiert die ganze Datei um, sortiert den Block und **löst die Kommentare von dem ab, was sie erklären**; gemessen 85 statt 9 Diff-Zeilen, was die Auditierbarkeit zerstört, auf der das ganze Deploy=Commit-Design ruht. Stattdessen `tools/deploy/gitops-image-bump.sh` (awk, nur `newTag:`-Zeilen im `images:`-Block) mit einem **Guard, der die eigene Ausgabe zurückliest und verweigert, sobald eine andere Zeile abwiche**. (2) Idempotent: gleicher sha = **kein Leer-Commit** (exit 2, Job grün). (3) **Ohne `GITOPS_PUSH_TOKEN` überspringt der Job sauber grün** (das `secrets`-Kontextobjekt ist in `if:` nicht verfügbar, die Prüfung steht deshalb im `run:`), damit der Zielpfad vor dem Secret mergebar ist. (4) Die Push-Schleife rebased NICHT - beide konkurrierenden Läufe fassen exakt dieselben Zeilen an; sie leitet bei jedem der drei Versuche vom frischen `origin/main` neu ab, was per Konstruktion konfliktfrei ist. Beweise (Docker-frei, im `deploy.yaml`-Test-Gate als Matrix-Leg `gitops-tag-bump`): `tools/deploy/test-gitops-image-bump.sh` (Edit gegen die committete Kopie des echten Overlays in `tools/deploy/testdata/`) und `tools/deploy/test-gitops-bump-workflow.sh` (führt den **echten `run:`-Text aus beiden Workflows** gegen ein lokales bare-Repo aus: Lockstep, Skip, Commit-Form, Idempotenz, verlorenes Push-Rennen, Form-Guard). Operator-Anleitung: [`docs/deploy.md`](docs/deploy.md) „gitops-Tag-Bump (CI-Zielpfad)".
- **Secrets** template: [`.env.prod.example`](.env.prod.example) (copied to `/srv/docker/voltpilot/.env`; `${VAR:?}` aborts the deploy if blank). Every required secret has an `openssl rand` hint. The device mTLS certs are staged on the VPS out of band (never in the repo/images); `SPRING_PROFILES_ACTIVE=local` seeds the demo tenants for a first look - blank it (and drop the demo realm users) for a real launch.
- **Host/domain are placeholders** throughout (captain fills `DOMAIN`, the VPS host, and the secrets later). Nothing here needs live infra to validate: `docker compose -f docker-compose.prod.yml config` and the workflow YAML both parse.

### Einen ROTEN CI-Lauf untersuchen (Forgejo 13.0.4)

Zwei Dinge, die jede Untersuchung sonst in die falsche Richtung schickt:

- **Welches Leg rot war, sagt AUSSCHLIESSLICH `GET /api/v1/repos/mamotec/voltpilot-ems/actions/tasks`.** Auf dieser Forgejo-Version antworten `actions/runs/{id}/jobs`, `actions/jobs/{id}/logs` und `actions/runs/{id}/logs` **allesamt 404** - es gibt über die API KEINE Protokolle, nur den Ausgang je Task. `actions/tasks` liefert je Task `name` („Test (api)"), `status`, `head_sha`, `run_number` und `run_started_at`/`updated_at`; deren Differenz IST die Dauer. Beachten: `actions/runs/{run}` nimmt die **API-Run-Id** (z. B. 1538), die Weboberfläche zeigt die **`run_number`** (183) - die beiden sind verschieden, und mit der falschen bekommt man 404. Basic-Auth mit dem Git-Credential (`git credential fill`) genügt für die API; die WEB-Oberfläche lässt sich damit NICHT anmelden (ein PAT ist kein Web-Passwort), also führt auch der Browser nicht an die Protokolle.
