Vier Dinge, die man kennen muss: (1) für einen noch nicht begonnenen Slot ist jeder gespeicherte Lauf älter, das Ende der Antwort IST also der aktuelle Plan (samt seiner Slots von morgen, die der Film eingeklappt lässt) — nur der LAUFENDE Slot kann von `latest` abweichen, wenn ein Re-Plan mitten in der Viertelstunde landete; (2) das Fenster hat bewusst **keine obere Grenze** (Zeilen existieren nur bis zum Horizont des jüngsten Laufs, `time >= dayStart` begrenzt sich also selbst); (3) **lauf-bezogene Fakten** (planId, bankedValueEur, socStart/socEnd, peakTargetKw, fallback14a) beschreiben EINEN Lauf und sind in dieser Lesart **null** — `generatedAt`/`deviceId` benennen den jüngsten BEITRAGENDEN Lauf, `savingsEur` summiert die gesplicten Slots; (4) ein Slot, den kein Lauf ex ante geplant hat, hat schlicht keine Zeile — ein Tag, dessen Optimierer erst mittags lief, beginnt ehrlich mittags. **SQL-Falle:** `time` ist ein `col_name_keyword`, deshalb ist die Tabelle in dieser einen Abfrage **aliasiert** (`s.generated_at <= s.time`) — ein nacktes `time` RECHTS eines Vergleichs liebäugelt mit der Typ-Literal-Grammatik; überall sonst steht es nur links. Beweise: `PortalApiTest.scheduleDayModeSplicesTheDayFromTheRunsInForce` (echte DB, drei Läufe: gestern-Abend besitzt den frühen Morgen, ein Re-Plan INNERHALB der Viertelstunde ändert sie nicht rückwirkend, der Slot danach folgt dem neuen Lauf, Morgen reist mit, Lauf-Felder null, `latest` unverändert, ein Tag ohne frühe Läufe beginnt später, 400/RLS) + das Docker-freie `ScheduleModeTest`. Portal-Seite (abgehakte Phasen, Ehrlichkeits-Satz, Fail-soft) in `frontend/portal/AGENTS.md`.
- **Compose**: own **`optimize` profile** (not `feeds` - it consumes the feeds and publishes MQTT; not `edge` - it is cloud-side): `docker compose --profile feeds --profile optimize up -d --build` (without the feeds every site is skipped until prices land). Prod: the `optimization` image joins the pulled set in `docker-compose.prod.yml`. Env: `POSTGRES_*` (trusted backend role), `MQTT_HOST/PORT` (+ optional `MQTT_USERNAME/PASSWORD`), `OPTIMIZER_INTERVAL_SECONDS` (900), `OPTIMIZER_HORIZON_HOURS` (24), plus the Stage-1 tunables `OPTIMIZER_WEAR_COST_CT_PER_KWH` (4.0) / `OPTIMIZER_GRID_LIMIT_MAX_AGE_MINUTES` (60) / `OPTIMIZER_SOC_MAX_AGE_MINUTES` (120), the Stage-2 `OPTIMIZER_EEG_RATES_JSON` (feste-Vergütung schedule override) and the Stage-3 `OPTIMIZER_TERMINAL_VALUE_QUANTILE` (0.3) / `OPTIMIZER_TERMINAL_VALUE_CT_PER_KWH` (unset = derive per plan).
- **Tests** (offline, no Docker needed): `tests/test_solver.py` (economic behavior on synthetic curves: charge-cheap/discharge-expensive, zero savings on flat, spread-below-losses stays idle, power/SoC/grid-limit compliance, efficiency in the SoC dynamics, no simultaneous charge+discharge at negative prices, infeasible-cap fallback, and the Phase-3 curtailment economics - curtails at negative prices with a full battery, NEVER at positive prices, engages exactly in the negative half of a mixed curve, makes a tight export cap feasible while staying minimal, exists in the fallback build; note the negative-price tests tolerate battery cycling: paid import covering round-trip losses is genuinely profitable under symmetric spot pricing, not a bug - and these tests double as the P1 regression proof, since a default `OptimizationInput` without import/export series IS the symmetric model), `tests/test_pricing.py` (P1 unit: import per tarif_art, DV premium incl. §51 suspension/missing-month/merchant-mode guard, feste-Vergütung lookup incl. blending/degression/§51a/expiry/env override, Berlin month mapping, baseline economics, gather_inputs wiring over a fake psycopg), `tests/test_objective.py` (P1 through the solver: the captain's §2.2 reference scenario - PV to battery while the load imports cheap grid power, evening discharge; never import+export simultaneously even when export value > import price; the §2.3 retail-vs-spot-settled contrast pair on identical physics - retail self-consumes without export, spot-settled DV exports past its own load; the F1-Experiment-4 hold-for-the-late-load fix incl. the symmetric contrast; flat retail tariff kills grid arbitrage (F7); feste-Vergütung plant never curtails at negative spot while the DV twin does (F6); premium revenue in cost_eur/baseline; EEG never grid-charges under a juicy premium), `tests/test_contract.py` (payload validates against the frozen schema incl. formats; curtailing slots carry `pv_limit_kw >= 0`, non-curtailing slots OMIT it so pre-Phase-3 payloads stay byte-identical), `tests/test_engine.py` (cycle orchestration with fakes), `tests/test_fallback.py` (persistence reuse), `tests/test_active_model.py` (shadow-mode regression: only the active model's forecast rows are consumed; the promotion env flip switches them), `tests/test_wear.py` (wear threshold band, per-asset override beats default, persisted per-slot wear economics, zero-wear opt-out), `tests/test_freshness.py` (stale grid_limit → no cap + flagged, stale SoC → default, env-tunable windows, NULL asset wear → platform default), `tests/test_config.py` (env tunables fail loudly on garbage), `tests/test_terminal_value.py` (P3: the F3 scenario discharges its full battery into the evening peak and waits for it, a cheap end-of-horizon tail is held while the explicit-V_end=0 contrast dumps, merchant sells an early peak without a forced buy-back, flat-curve-stays-idle regression, the V_end derivation incl. quantile/override envs), `tests/test_backup_reserve.py` (P11: the floor arithmetic incl. below-reserve start + 100% pin, plans never below the configured reserve at any price, NULL = old 5% behavior, reserve-hard-vs-V_end-soft). NOTE for solver tests since P3: a plan may realize PRE-STORED energy at any price above the terminal anchor, so pure-cycling assertions must start at the SoC floor (`test_wear.py`/the small-spread test do). The api's `PortalApiTest.scheduleEndpointReturnsLatestPlanTenantScoped` proves the read path + RLS against real TimescaleDB/Keycloak.
- **Multi-entity co-optimizer basis (E4-Basis, v2 track; PR fm/vp2-e4-cooptimizer).** The solver generalization to N entities ships ALONGSIDE the untouched v1 path: `entities.py` (`CoOptimizationInput` with storages[]/producers[]/controllable_loads[] + the `from_v1_input` N=1 adapter + the `SitePlan` artifact; controllable loads are declared-but-empty-only until their E4 follow-up), `modules.py` (the former inline conditionals as DECLARED constraint/objective contributions selected per site - solar-only/§14a/feed-in/peak/reservation-stack; `build_co_model(..., modules=...)` is the strategy-node registration seam), `co_solver.py` (pure function, every v1 rule generalized by summation - ONE grid balance per connection point, per-entity SoC/binaries/wear/terminal value, EEG solar-only as a joint SUBSET constraint over the non-permitted storages). **The GOLDEN SUITE `tests/golden/` + `tests/test_golden_cooptimizer.py` is the cutover acceptance basis** - eight committed pilot-shaped scenarios (Excel reference day, EEG household, C&I peak shaving, DV negative prices, §14a incl. fallback build, kitchen-sink) solved through BOTH solvers and compared slot-by-slot (objective 1e-5 EUR, slots 1e-3 kW; fixtures are generator-pinned against drift) - read `tests/golden/README.md` BEFORE touching either solver, and never loosen its tolerances to make a change pass. **Schedule-2.0 shadow publisher** (`publisher_v2.py`): sites flagged via `VOLTPILOT_V2_PLAN_SITES` (env flip, default empty - no DB column, no site flagged in prod) additionally get a co-optimized per-entity plan retained on `ems/{t}/{s}/{d}/v2/plan` per `docs/contracts/v2/mqtt-schedule-2.0.schema.json` (E13a: v2 publishes, v1 controls - the 1.0 publisher is untouched for every site, a shadow failure only logs). D-8 discipline: `charge_from_grid_allowed` is ALWAYS explicit (absent = NOT allowed); a non-curtailing producer is OMITTED (release clears limits), a curtailing one keeps contiguity with `limit_pct: 100` no-ops. **DV-konformer Modus** = `CoOptimizationInput.dv_konform` (hard-disables grid-charge arbitrage for EVERY storage, most-restrictive-wins, consumption-side strategies stay active; mapping table onto the netzladen_erlaubt machinery in the `entities.py` docstring). Attribution groundwork (design only): `docs/attribution-three-pot.md` (green/grey/arbitrage three-pot extension of `EarningsRepository.arbitrageSplit` for the D6 Erlösbeteiligung - no earnings runtime change yet). Tests: `test_golden_cooptimizer.py`, `test_cooptimizer.py` (multi-entity: per-entity permissions, joint solar budget, DV-konform incl. peak-module-stays-active, efficiency-ordered cycling, curtailable-vs-must-run producers), `test_contract_v2.py` (payloads + the repo's v2 example fixtures validate, D-8 both values, release/no-op-limit rules, e2e kitchen-sink).

## Fahrplan-Warum backend (per-slot "why" facts: optimizer explain layer + schedule columns)

The optimizer computes + persists the honest per-slot decision facts of every plan (design scout `vp-fahrplan-why-design`, owner-approved D1-D6; the portal rendering is a separate later PR). **`voltpilot_optimization/explain.py`** runs AFTER `_extract_plan` on the still-live model: fix every binary to its MILP value AND relax its domain to `Reals` (BOTH required - `.fix()` alone leaves APPSI/HiGHS treating it as a MIP with no duals), fresh APPSI `Highs()` LP re-solve (~30-40 ms/cycle, objective drift <= 1e-13), then read the duals with the empirically verified sign conventions: **`λ_t = −dual(soc_dynamics[t])`** (value of a stored kWh at slot END, the "Wasserwert" - the EXACT number `SlotEconomics` previously approximated), **`π_t = dual(grid_balance[t])/dt`** (effective energy value at the connection point), **`μ_t = −dual(peak_epigraph[t]) − dual(peak_below_epigraph[t])`** (Leistungspreis allocation; Σ over slots+anchor = LP exactly, Σ ratchet duals = ratchet). Binding flags come from a primal-slack/bounds scan (no duals needed); the slot role is the §6 precedence rule-tree (`abregeln`/`reserve_halten`/`warten`/`pv_speichern`/`guenstig_laden`/`spitze_kappen`/`verkaufen`/`eigenverbrauch`), deadbands = the shared `schedule.ts`/`SlotEconomics` constants (third copy in explain.py - change together).

- **Safety invariants (non-negotiable):** the committed plan is BYTE-IDENTICAL with explain on/off (proven per golden scenario); the LP re-solve MUTATES the model so it runs strictly after plan extraction and its solution is never loaded back (`load_solution=False`, no `load_vars()`); ANY explain exception (incl. a garbage flag value) warn-logs and ships the plan with all why-fields None - explain can never sink a plan; flag `OPTIMIZER_EXPLAIN_ENABLED` (default true) is the instant off-switch; the why NEVER reaches MQTT (publisher builds payloads field-by-field). The Ersparnis-Simulation passes `optimize(..., explain_plan=False)` (365 solves/job would pay ~11 s for discarded fields).
- **Maintenance coupling (§11.6):** whoever adds a model constraint MUST extend `explain.KNOWN_CONSTRAINTS` + the binding scan/role tree - `explain()` REFUSES unmapped constraints (plan then ships why-less), and `test_explain.py`'s inventory guard fails at dev time.
- **Data contract (§5.1, api migration `V20260723030000` + mirror `infra/local/timescale/04-schedule.sql`):** typed nullable `schedule` columns `slot_role` TEXT, `slot_flags` TEXT (CSV; empty tuple persists NULL), `stored_value_ct_kwh` (λ, rounded 0.1 ct - degeneracy rounding), `grid_value_ct_kwh` (π), `peak_pressure_eur_kw` (μ), `fallback_14a` BOOLEAN (run-level, repeated per row - the terminal_value pattern; TRUE = advisory no-§14a build). NULL everywhere = pre-feature/explain-off - consumers render exactly today's view, never a fabricated explanation. Served additively on `ScheduleSlotDto`/`SchedulePlanDto` (customer `GET /sites/{id}/schedule`) and on the admin diagnostics, where the persisted exact `stored_value_ct_kwh` replaces the approximation (`storedEnergyValueIsApproximation` is false only when EVERY rendered slot carries it).
- **Portal consumer - "a 'nothing to do' must name its true cause or stay silent" (2026-07-27).** The Fahrplan's idle sentence used to be a blanket fallback asserting "die Preisunterschiede lohnen kein Laden und Entladen" while inspecting NO price - which is what hid the flat-tariff freeze above for as long as it lasted (idle over a 20 ct spread, blaming the spread). `schedule.ts` `idleReason` now derives it from these persisted facts - dominant `slot_role` of the IDLE slots, `reserve_backup`/`reserve_peak` flags, `stored_value_ct_kwh` vs the window's best price - and returns **null when the optimizer recorded nothing**, in which case both consumers (`planInsightParts` on the Fahrplan chart, `planSentence` on the Anlagen-Seite mini preview) state the observation and claim NO cause. An idle stretch under a role the portal vocabulary does not know yet also makes no claim, so extending the role set can never invent copy. Pinned by `src/schedule.test.ts`.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_explain.py` (stationarity identities η·λ=π+wear / λ=η·(π−wear) within the ε-tie-break tolerance, Σμ=LP+ratchet exact, role vectors per scenario, fallback marking, plan-byte-identical on/off, explain-failure-never-sinks, constraint-inventory guard); `PortalApiTest`/`AdminApiTest` pin the read side + null degradation. The golden suite is the primary safety gate and stays untouched.

## Price-aware in-slot trim: the cloud prices, the edge enforces (2026-07-30)

The 15-min setpoint stands for the whole quarter hour, but PV and house load move every second - so a forecast shortfall was silently covered from the GRID. Captain observation at Anlage Pilsting (30.07., 14:44): PV 15,3 · Haus 7,6 · Batterie lädt 11,1 · **Netzbezug 3,4 kW** at a 3,3 ct spot against a 22 ct evening, i.e. the purchase was economically RIGHT - which is exactly why "just charge the surplus" is the WRONG fix (that is the price-blind Eigenverbrauchslogik, the same category error as the fixed night-emptying bug with the sign flipped). The answer is CONDITIONAL, and the split is architectural law: **THE CLOUD DECIDES WHETHER A SLOT IS EXPENSIVE, THE EDGE ONLY LIMITS.** The edge evaluates no price, ever (one price truth - the `pricing.py`/`SlotEconomics` discipline).

- **The rule (`services/optimization/voltpilot_optimization/slot_trim.py`)**: the marginal willingness to pay for one more AC kWh charged in slot t is `WTP_t = eta*λ_t − wear`, where **λ_t is the model's OWN marginal value of stored energy** (the LP dual the explain layer already extracts and persists as `stored_value_ct_kwh`). A grid purchase for the battery is uneconomic iff `import_price_t > WTP_t + margin` (`SLOT_TRIM_MARGIN_CT_PER_KWH` 0.5 ct - it swallows the 0.1 ct degeneracy rounding of the persisted λ AND keeps a hairline case untrimmed). A plain threshold ("above X ct") was deliberately rejected: it is wrong at the next price regime, whereas this test IS the model's optimality condition and self-adjusts. Sanity check on the observed case: WTP ≈ 28 ct ≫ 14,6 ct import → NOT uneconomic → Pilsting keeps doing exactly what it did.
- **Emission** (`solver._charge_from_surplus_only`, stamped in `_with_explanation` next to the why-fields; `PlanSlot.charge_from_surplus_only`): only on slots whose plan actually COMMANDS a charge (the `pv_limit_kw` discipline - every other payload stays byte-identical), plus a consistency guard that never flags a slot the plan itself grid-charges in. λ comes from explain, so `OPTIMIZER_EXPLAIN_ENABLED=false` (or the new `OPTIMIZER_SLOT_TRIM_ENABLED=false` kill switch) degrades to today's behavior.
- **Contract**: the OPTIONAL per-slot boolean **`charge_from_surplus_only`** in `docs/contracts/mqtt-schedule.schema.json` = the edge's DUTY to clamp commanded CHARGE to the MEASURED surplus `max(pv − load, 0)`. Distinct from the top-level `grid_charge_allowed`: that one is REGULATORY (EEG, charge ≤ measured PV PRODUCTION, house may import in parallel) and the Go edge fails SAFE on it; this one is ECONOMIC (charge ≤ measured SURPLUS, so the battery causes no import at all) and is deliberately **FAIL-OPEN** - a missing economic hint must never reshape dispatch, and an inferred restriction would destroy real arbitrage. Most restrictive wins; neither ever widens anything.
- **Edge (`edge-app/core/internal/guards/slottrim.go` `PriceTrimmer`, applied in `applySetpoint` after Clamp/arbitration and before the peak guard)**: restrict-only on charge, asymmetric hysteresis (engage immediately - a passing cloud is what must not be bought; release only after the plan setpoint has comfortably fit the surplus for `TrimReleaseDwell` 90 s), the applied cap follows a SHRINKING surplus at once and a growing one only in `TrimStepKw` steps (stable register writes). **Safety by algebra:** after a trim the predicted grid power is `max(load − pv, 0) ≥ 0`, so the site is never pushed into export → the §14a export bound and any feed-in cap are untouchable, and the import bound was applied to a value the trim only reduces further; the result lives strictly in `[0, kw]`, so the rated band + SoC window hold trivially and FK3 (looser) can never be widened. Unknown pv/load → INACTIVE (never regulate blind - the economic-guard convention, cf. PeakShave). The self-consumption fallback follows pv − load, i.e. the surplus itself, so the trim is a no-op there.
- **A trim is a DELIBERATE limitation, never a failed write:** the TRIMMED value is what gets published, so the register readback matches it and the PR #280 confirmation logic cannot read it as "Sollwert nicht übernommen". It is surfaced as its own state (`state.TrimInfo` → the `:8484` control card's reason line), because an unnamed limitation reads as a defect.
- **Tests:** `test_slot_trim.py` (the rule as economic properties with the captain's live numbers as the anchor; through the real solver: a flat-retail plant flags EVERY planned charge slot while a cheap-night purchase for an expensive evening stays untouched; flag off / explain off; setpoints byte-identical with the trim off), `test_contract.py` (additive/omitted + the committed fixtures both ways + fixture-vs-publisher), Go `guards/slottrim_test.go` (surplus hold, no-surplus stops instead of buying, discharge untouched, blind-inactive, FK3-tighter, §14a-export-safe, no flapping, wiggle-stable cap) + `agent/slot_trim_test.go` (expensive/cheap/legacy/stale/no-reading through `applySetpoint`, published==state) + `plan/plan_test.go` (explicit-true only, active-slot semantics, the committed fixtures), edge JS `web/jstest/ui.test.js` (the reason line), portal `control.test.ts`.

## In-slot load following: the DISCHARGE mirror of the trim (2026-07-30, P1)

The same quarter-hour gap with the sign flipped, and the one that cost real money - the Pilsting NIGHT (scout report `firstmate/data/vp-netzbezug-nacht-s3`, §6 P1). The plan discharges exactly what it FORECASTS the house to draw, so the forecast error is settled at the grid **in whichever direction it points** - both halves measured live on 2026-07-30: 21:22 MESZ plan −4,332 kW into a **7,117 kW** house = **2,79 kW GEKAUFT at ~32,5 ct** while the battery sat at **77 % SoC** (and the plan, believing the night smaller than it was, carried ~15 kWh unused into the morning, **≈ 4,9 EUR in ONE night**); 23:12 the mirror image, plan −6,7 kW into a **5,1 kW** house = **1,4 kW EXPORTIERT at ~21 ct** while that kWh was worth ~32,5 ct as avoided import hours later. Same architectural law as the trim: **the cloud decides the economics of the slot, the edge only enforces against MEASURED values** (the edge evaluates no price, ever). Since P1b (2026-07-30, captain go) the enforcement is **BIDIRECTIONAL**: in a flagged slot the target is `Entladung = max(load − pv, 0)`, i.e. **grid ≈ 0**, reached by raising a short discharge AND by limiting an overshooting one.

- **The rule (same module `slot_trim.py`)**: covering one AC kWh of house load from the battery COSTS `λ_t/eta + wear` (delivering 1 AC kWh drains `1/eta` stored kWh) and SAVES `import_price_t`, so it is economic iff `import_price_t > λ_t/eta + wear + margin` (the shared `SLOT_TRIM_MARGIN_CT_PER_KWH`). This is STRICTLY tighter than the charge-side test, so **the two duties can never contradict each other** (`test_load_follow.py` pins that as a property sweep). Again the model's own optimality condition, not a threshold.
- **Emission** (`solver._cover_load_from_battery`, stamped in `_with_explanation`; `PlanSlot.cover_load_from_battery`): only where the plan actually COMMANDS a discharge **and** plans NO grid exchange worth the name - **`|grid_kw| <= PLANNED_GRID_EXCHANGE_DEADBAND_KW` (0,05 kW), the "Netz ≈ 0" kink of role `eigenverbrauch`**. **BOTH sides are excluded, each for its own reason** (P1b, 2026-07-30): a planned IMPORT is a deliberate cheap-hour purchase (role `warten`) the duty must never undo, and a planned EXPORT is a deliberate sale (role `verkaufen`) that the BIDIRECTIONAL edge enforcement would otherwise cut back to zero grid - the edge receives only the setpoint, never the plan's own forecast grid power, so **the trade/overshoot distinction has to be made HERE**, where both numbers exist. That one condition is what keeps the price arbitrage untouched in both directions. Own kill-switch `OPTIMIZER_LOAD_FOLLOW_ENABLED` (deliberately NOT shared with the trim - the two push the setpoint in opposite directions on a control path, so an operator must be able to stop one without losing the other); no explain layer → no flag.
- **Contract**: the OPTIONAL per-slot boolean **`cover_load_from_battery`** = the edge's duty to TRACK commanded DISCHARGE to the MEASURED deficit `max(load − pv, 0)` - raise a short one, limit an overshooting one, **hard floor at zero discharge** (never a charge, never a direction flip). FAIL-OPEN like its twin (an inferred duty would be the price-blind Eigenverbrauchslogik). NO payload change for P1b - only the description/`x-failsafe` were written forward, `schema_version` stays 1.0 and the cloud is untouched (the flag's "Netz ≈ 0, nicht handeln" semantics already carried both directions). Fixtures `mqtt-schedule.valid.cover-load.json` + `…invalid.cover-load-not-boolean.json`.
- **Edge (`guards/loadfollow.go` `LoadFollower`, in `applySetpoint` right after the trim, before the peak guard)**: the DEEPEN half **CALLS `PeakShave(kw, 0, …)`** rather than re-deriving the bounds, so rated band / SoC floor / never-raise come from ONE proven piece of arithmetic. **Safety by algebra (the ONE invariant, both directions):** the correction only ever moves the predicted grid power **TOWARD zero - never past it, never further away**, so the site is never pushed into export (§14a export bound + feed-in cap untouchable) and the import is never raised above what entered the guard (that bound was applied to it). Where it bites the BATTERY stops contributing: exactly 0 with a house deficit, the remaining PV surplus without one (absorbing that would take a CHARGE = a price decision this guard must not take). The LIMIT half needs no bound of its own (a shrinking discharge magnitude cannot escape the rated band or SoC floor, never touches the SoC ceiling or the EEG solar-only clamp) and can never undo the later peak guard (it lands at predicted import 0 ≤ every allowance ≥ 0). A commanded CHARGE is never flipped. The plan-carried **peak reserve raises the SoC floor of the DEEPEN half only** (ordinary load covering is exactly what the reserve must survive - the fallback's rule; peak DEFENSE may still go below it); limiting only ever PRESERVES stored energy. Unknown pv/load → INACTIVE. The self-consumption fallback already follows pv − load, so it is a no-op there. **Hysteresis is symmetric around the zero-grid target** (engage immediately when `|predicted| > FollowEngageMarginKw` either way, release only after `FollowReleaseDwell` of the plan's OWN value tracking within the margin; the engaged state carries across a direction change). **Deliberate deviation from the trim: no step-limited follower on the applied value** - both directions are load following, and holding a stale deeper discharge while the deficit SHRINKS is precisely the 23:12 symptom.
- **The trade/overshoot distinction lives in the CLOUD, never on the edge:** the edge receives only the setpoint, so it cannot tell a DELIBERATE export from a forecast overshoot - which is exactly why the emission rule above excludes both trade directions. An UNMARKED slot is byte-for-byte untouched on the edge, so a sell window (role `verkaufen`) and a cheap-hour purchase (role `warten`) are both safe from the correction. Safety does NOT rest on that, though: a device paired with an OLDER cloud can still see a marked slot planning a small export; limiting it then HOLDS that energy (worth ≥ the water value, re-dispatched by the next 15-min replan) - a bounded, self-correcting deferral, unlike the unpriced export the correction exists to stop.
- **A correction is DELIBERATE IN EITHER DIRECTION, never a failed write** (the same PR #280 lesson): the FOLLOWED value is published, so the readback matches it. Surfaced as `state.FollowInfo` (with `direction` = `deepen`|`reduce`) → `control.js VPControl.deriveFollow` → the same `#ctrlReason` line, naming the direction ("… – Entladung angehoben" / "Folgt dem gemessenen Hausverbrauch … – Entladung begrenzt", each with its OWN honest cause, NORMAL mode). An older device reporting no direction gets the neutral wording - never claim one it did not send.
- **Tests:** `test_load_follow.py` (rule properties incl. the never-contradict sweep and the symmetric deadband; through the REAL solver the report's night curve marks its `eigenverbrauch` slots while a cheap-hour purchase AND a deliberate 28-kW export stay unmarked - the export case is deliberately NON-VACUOUS: it asserts those `verkaufen` slots' own economics WOULD have flagged them, so it proves the guard and not a rule that would have refused them anyway, and the same plan still flags its `eigenverbrauch` tail; both switches independent; setpoints byte-identical with the flag off), `test_golden_cooptimizer.py` (unchanged), `test_contract.py`, Go `guards/loadfollow_test.go` + `agent/load_follow_test.go` + `plan/plan_test.go`, edge JS `web/jstest/ui.test.js`. Verified end to end on real bytes: the real solver's payload for the report's night → `plan.Parse` → `applySetpoint` turns the plan's −4,302 kW into **−7,087 kW**, predicted grid **0,000 kW** (live: +2,755 kW). P1b adds the mirror at the same level - through `applySetpoint` the live 23:12 constellation (−6,7 kW plan, 5,1 kW house) publishes **−5,1 kW**, predicted grid **0,000 kW** (live: −1,4 kW export), and the COMMITTED contract fixture (`mqtt-schedule.valid.cover-load.json`, read by path) is limited from −4,332 to **−2,0 kW** against a 2-kW house; the unmarked 30-kW sell window stays −30,0 kW.

## In-slot surplus absorption: the only duty that RAISES a charge (2026-08-02)

The third and last member of the in-slot duty family, and the one the other two structurally could not cover - the trim only ever LOWERS a charge, the follower only ever acts on a DISCHARGE, so **nothing could put an unforecast PV surplus INTO the battery**. Captain observation at Anlage Pilsting (02.08., ~10:41): PV 23,9 · Haus 4,3 · **Netz-EINSPEISUNG 16,6 kW** at a negative spot price, hour after hour, with the battery at **7 % SoC** - while the cockpit said "die PV wird gedrosselt". Report `firstmate/data/vp-pilsting-abregeln` §5b: the morning's real money is NOT the unexecuted curtailment (~0,1-0,7 €) but the **unstored surplus** (~2-10 €/day, every sunny negative-price day). Root cause: the solver charges only the FORECAST surplus (`charge ≤ pv_forecast − curtail`) and there is no nowcast, so every 15-min re-plan repeats the same too-small number. Same architectural law as its siblings: **THE CLOUD PRICES, THE EDGE ENFORCES** - the edge evaluates no price, ever.

- **The rule (`services/optimization/voltpilot_optimization/slot_trim.py`)**: storing one more kWh beats selling it iff **`η·λ_t − wear > export_value_t + margin`** (shared `SLOT_TRIM_MARGIN_CT_PER_KWH`), where λ is again the model's OWN LP dual (`stored_value_ct_kwh`). Note the marginal value here is **UNCLAMPED** (`marginal_storage_value_ct_kwh`), deliberately NOT the trim's `willingness_to_pay_ct_kwh`: that one floors at 0, which against a NEGATIVE export value would claim storing is worth it even where the wear spent exceeds the giveaway avoided. At a negative price the test is satisfied as soon as the water value covers the wear - the report's own reading.
- **Emission** (`solver._charge_surplus_to_battery`, stamped in `_with_explanation`; `PlanSlot.charge_surplus_to_battery`; own kill-switch `OPTIMIZER_SURPLUS_CHARGE_ENABLED`, passed through in BOTH composes): the economic test, PLUS the plan intends no deliberate SALE (`grid_kw >= -0.05 kW`, or the slot CURTAILS - which by definition prefers not to export), PLUS the plan's own SoC trajectory still has headroom (`slot END SoC < soc_max − 0.1 kWh`; an unfulfillable duty is never published). Deliberately NOT gated on the plan commanding a charge - the whole point is the slot planning 0,0 kW.
- **⚠ DELIBERATE DEVIATION from the report's literal formula 5b, argued in the rule's docstring:** 5b copied P1b's two-sided `|grid_kw| ≈ 0` exclusion, which would have killed the money case - under FK3 PV-bus semantics an ordinary EEG charging slot plans `grid_kw = +load` ("battery takes the PV, house buys its load"), measured at 4,3 kW on the very morning in question (~4,7 €/h at stake in ONE slot). A planned IMPORT is therefore NOT excluded, and it is structurally safe to allow: this duty is **UNIDIRECTIONAL** (it only RAISES, and only up to the measured surplus = predicted grid exactly 0), so it bites ONLY where reality is EXPORTING and can never touch a deliberate cheap-hour purchase, which charges MORE than the surplus by construction. That asymmetry to P1b - whose enforcement is BIDIRECTIONAL and therefore genuinely could undo a purchase - is the whole argument; do not "restore the symmetry".
- **Edge (`guards/surpluscharge.go` `SurplusCharger`, in `applySetpoint` after the trim AND the follower, before the peak guard)**: raise-only, and only on a NON-NEGATIVE command (never a direction flip - which is also what keeps it disjoint from the follower). **Because it RAISES, its target is re-run through the authoritative `guards.Clamp`** (rated band, SoC ceiling, EEG solar-only charge, §14a envelope) instead of a hand-rolled bound - the same technique the follower's deepen half uses with `PeakShave`, so it is impossible for it to write past a guard or widen one. **Safety by algebra:** bounded by the measured surplus, predicted grid = `load + min(kw, surplus) − pv ≤ 0`, so it can never create or raise an IMPORT (§14a import bound + the peak quarter-hour target untouched) and only ever moves an export TOWARD zero (§14a export bound + feed-in cap hold a fortiori). Unknown pv/load → INACTIVE. The self-consumption fallback already charges pv − load, so it is a no-op there. Hysteresis mirrors the trim (engage immediately, release after `AbsorbReleaseDwell`; the applied level follows a shrinking surplus at once, a growing one in `AbsorbStepKw` steps).
- **A raised setpoint is DELIBERATE, never a failed write:** the ABSORBED value is published, so the readback matches it. Surfaced as `state.AbsorbInfo` → `control.js VPControl.deriveAbsorb` → the same `#ctrlReason` line ("Lädt den gemessenen Solarüberschuss … – Ladung angehoben", NORMAL mode) and as the heartbeat `execution.mode: "absorb"` (checked FIRST in `executionSummary` because it runs LAST in the setpoint chain, so where it bit its value is the published one).
- **Cloud-side ingest of `mode: "absorb"` is deliberately NOT in this increment** (the api `ControlStatusListener`'s strict mode filter DROPS an unknown mode, so an un-updated portal degrades to its generic wording - the documented graceful path), and neither is persisting the flag into the `schedule` table like the Fahrplan-concept PR 4 did for the two older duties. Both are the natural follow-ups.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_surplus_charge.py` (rule properties with the live numbers as the anchor, the unclamped-vs-clamped divergence, the import-side deviation, curtail disjunct, SoC headroom; through the REAL solver: the under-forecast morning is flagged, a full battery is not, **a deliberate export stays unmarked NON-VACUOUSLY** - those slots' economics WOULD have flagged them - and **the honest hold of H2 never fires**, also non-vacuously on the economics; all three switches independent; setpoints byte-identical with the flag off), `test_contract.py` (fixture-vs-publisher + omit-unless-true + both fixtures), Go `guards/surpluscharge_test.go` + `agent/surplus_charge_test.go` + `plan/plan_test.go`, edge JS `web/jstest/ui.test.js`. The GOLDEN suite is untouched and green. Verified end to end on real bytes: the real solver's payload for the under-forecast morning → `plan.Parse` → `applySetpoint` turns the plan's **6,000 kW** into **19,600 kW**, predicted grid **0,000 kW** (was −14,7 kW of export at a negative price), heartbeat `mode: "absorb"`; the COMMITTED fixture (`mqtt-schedule.valid.absorb-surplus.json`, read by path) does the same from its 0,0 kW slot.

## Dynamische Einspeisebegrenzung: der Netzpunkt wird GEREGELT, nicht nur geplant (2026-08-06)

Der Echtzeit-Wächter, der die Einspeisegrenze am Netzverknüpfungspunkt hält - die Funktion, die in Pilsting bis dahin eine **kundeneigene Loxone** übernahm (sie gibt dem Wechselrichter die Wirkleistung vor und verrechnet dabei die Wallboxen: lädt ein Auto, darf mehr erzeugt werden; wird es abgesteckt, muss die Erzeugung SOFORT runter - „sonst schiesst der drüber wenn ein Auto abgesteckt wird"). VoltPilot **plante** die Grenze längst (`site.max_feed_in_kw` ist seit FK1 eine harte Export-Kappe im Solver), **regelte** sie aber nicht: die Anlagen-Kappe, die die Box auf die Fronius-Einheiten aufteilt, kam aus dem 15-Minuten-PLAN, ein abgestecktes Auto blieb also bis zum nächsten Re-Plan unbemerkt. Für den Netz-BEZUG existierte der Regelkreis seit PS-3 (`guards/peakguard.go`); dies ist sein Gegenstück auf der Einspeise-Seite, nach demselben Muster gebaut. Edge-Details + Betreiber-Ablauf: `edge-app/AGENTS.md` „Dynamische Einspeisebegrenzung", `edge-app/nodered/FRONIUS.md` §6d, Umstellungs-Choreografie in `edge-app/nodered/CONTROL-BENCH.md`.

- **Cloud-Beitrag: EIN additives Feld.** `SchedulePlan.max_feed_in_kw` ist ein reines Durchreichen des Stammdatums, das der Solver ohnehin als Export-Kappe bindet (nie ein GELÖSTER Wert - ein gelöster Export-Peak läge unter der echten Grenze und drosselte die Anlage still), veröffentlicht als OPTIONALes Top-Level-`grid_export_limit_kw` im **frozen** `mqtt-schedule`-Kontrakt (`schema_version` bleibt 1.0; Präzedenzfall ist `grid_import_limit_kw` der Lastspitzenkappung). **Ohne gepflegte Grenze bleibt das Feld weg**, jede andere Nutzlast ist byte-identisch, und der Wächter bleibt inaktiv - eine Grenze wird nie erfunden.
- **Der Regelkreis läuft auf der BOX** (`edge-app/core/internal/guards/exportlimit.go`), gegen die GEMESSENE Netzleistung: Haus, Wallboxen und Batterie sind damit automatisch mitverrechnet, weil sie in dieser Messung schon stecken - das ist der ganze Vorteil gegenüber der Planung. Das Ergebnis ist eine ANLAGEN-Kappe, dieselbe Größe wie `pv_limit_kw`, die der bestehende Abregel-Executor unverändert aufteilt; die beiden komponieren **most-restrictive-wins**, der Wächter lockert eine geplante Drosselung (Negativpreis/FK1) nie und rührt §14a/EEG/SoC/Nennband nicht an. Die Batterie ist ausdrücklich KEIN Stellglied (Überschuss zu speichern ist eine Optimierer-Entscheidung, siehe „In-slot surplus absorption").
- **⚠ Die Fail-Safe-Regel ist hier die UMKEHRUNG aller anderen Guards.** Ökonomische Guards gelten „blind ⇒ inaktiv"; bei einer Compliance-Grenze darf blind nicht „unbegrenzt" heißen. Also Stufen: kurze Messlücke ⇒ die letzte Kappe HALTEN, längere ⇒ auf eine sichere statische Kappe (`Grenze − befohlene Entladung`, hält bei jedem Hausverbrauch) zusammenziehen, nie gemessen ⇒ sofort diese Kappe. Nie eine Freigabe.
- **Das Stellglied-Tor bleibt unverändert**: geschrieben wird nur über den bestehenden Abregel-Pfad, also mit der First-Light-Freigabe JE EINHEIT und dem Not-Aus. Ein nicht freigegebener Wechselrichter wird nicht beschrieben - dann ist der Wächter nachweislich **wirkungslos** und sagt das laut (`:8484`, Log, Herzschlag `curtailment.export_guard`), damit niemand sich auf einen Schutz verlässt, den es nicht gibt. Der Herzschlag braucht KEINE api-Änderung (der Listener liest ihn als `JsonNode`); ein Portal-Lesepfad ist die natürliche Folgearbeit.
- **Beweise:** `edge-app/core/internal/guards/exportlimit_test.go` (abgestecktes Auto, wieder angestecktes Auto, Wolke, Messausfall in allen Stufen, sichere Kappe, jeder Zustand mit Grund), `internal/agent/export_limit_test.go` (Verdrahtung + Komposition + die vier Wirkungslos-Fälle + Herzschlag == Karte), `curtail-lease.e2e.test.js` EINSPEISE-WACHE (echter In-Process-SunSpec-Server: fallende Kappe landet SOFORT im Register, Aufteilung, unfreigegebene Einheit schreibt nichts), `services/optimization/tests/test_contract.py` + `test_solver.py` (additiv/weggelassen, Durchreichen), `plan_test.go` (die eingecheckten Kontrakt-Fixtures per Pfad).

## E2 flow platform core (edge): arbitration runtime + flowc compiler + flow deployment

The v2 track's edge runtime slice (branch fm/vp2-e2-flow-core; contracts `docs/contracts/v2/` are BINDING - E2's additive clarifications are logged in that README's decision log). Details + proof map in `edge-app/AGENTS.md` §"E2 flow platform core"; the essentials:
- **Desired arbitration** (`edge-app/core/internal/desired` + `agent/arbitration.go`): flows publish WISHES on `edge/entities/{id}/desired`; the core arbitrates (D-4 classes, D-6 holder-keeps conflicts, D-5 override ≤ 4 h, D-7 required TTL), clamps the winner through the per-entity registry guard chain COMPOSED with the v1 device limits (most restrictive wins; stage-attributed reasons via `guards.ClampTraced`), and ALONE publishes the retained `…/command` + arbitration events. The winning holder also drives the physical v1 `edge/setpoint` write; the registry failsafe stays with the v1 fallback computation. The v2 plan (`internal/plan2`, retained `…/v2/plan`, v1 staleness semantics) injects class-`market` desires - a fresh v1 plan keeps controlling the battery (shadow phase, "v1 controls").
- **flowc** (`edge-app/nodered/flowc`): deterministic flow-graph→Node-RED compiler (catalog-only nodes, whitelisted template codegen, `@vp-flow` tab marker, RFC 8785 `content_hash`). CROSS-LANGUAGE LOCKSTEP: `flowc/canonicalize.js` ⟷ `edge-app/core/internal/flowdeploy/jcs.go` share `flowc/jcs-vectors.json`, and the committed `flowc/testdata/*.artifact.json` must verify through the Go deployer - never edit one side alone. vp-palette 0.2.0 adds `vp-desired`/`vp-entity-read`/`vp-feed`/`vp-notify`.
- **Flow deployment** (`edge-app/core/internal/flowdeploy` + `agent/flows.go`): retained `…/v2/flows` set → hash/semver-gate/capability verification (palette version read LIVE from NR `GET /nodes`) → `@vp-flow` tabs via the NR Admin API → heartbeat `flows` acks; persistence + periodic reconcile self-heal. Core env: `VP_NODERED_ADMIN_URL`/`VP_NODERED_USER`/`VP_NODERED_PASSWORD` (unset = honest `error` acks naming the setting).
- **Reseed coexistence (D-12)**: `edge-app/nodered/reseed-merge-flows.js` + the entrypoint merge preserve `@vp-flow` tabs across template re-seeds, proven against the REAL image (`reseed-flows.docker.test.sh` - the #117/#119 discipline).
- **September-Gate rig**: `edge-app/test/e2e-v2-compose.sh` (own project/ports; pre-builds via plain `docker build` for old-buildx hosts) proves registry push → flowc artifact → NR → desired → arbitration → guards → certified sunspec write → register readback, plus clamp/conflict/override/staleness/coexistence (spec P1-P7).

## Portal-Admin optimizer surface (diagnostics + config + what-if, `services/api` `optimizer/` package)

Backend for the admin "understand + tune the optimizer" GUI (design: firstmate `vp-admin-optimizer-ui-design`). FOUR `platform-admin`-gated routes under `/api/v1/admin/sites/{siteId}/...` (`AdminOptimizerController`), all reading/writing THROUGH the RLS-scoped app datasource via the `X-Tenant-Id` switcher (no BYPASSRLS; no tenant selected / wrong tenant = 404). Two of them look alike and must not be confused: a config PUT changes what the optimizer's NEXT 15-min cycle reads and triggers no solve, while `optimizer-what-if` (§4.3, see its own section below) solves IMMEDIATELY and commits NOTHING.

- **`GET .../optimizer-diagnostics?generatedAt=&date=`** (default = latest run; unknown timestamp 404; `availableRuns` lists ONE Europe/Berlin day's runs - `date=YYYY-MM-DD` picks the day and without `generatedAt` shows that day's newest run, a day without runs = empty well-formed body, never 404; `firstRunDate`/`lastRunDate` bound the UI date picker - the hypertable keeps every run, the former newest-30 list only reached ~7.5 h back): the run's `schedule` slots plus the per-slot €-decomposition in ct/kWh - `solverPriceCtKwh` (bare spot), `importPriceCtKwh`/`exportValueCtKwh` (the REAL P1 pricing), `wearCostEur`/`wearCostCtKwh` (from the PERSISTED `schedule.wear_cost_eur`, real P2 - not an estimate), `valueOfStoredEnergyCtKwh` (forward best-use price × one-way η minus half-wear - an APPROXIMATION, flagged run-level as `storedEnergyValueIsApproximation`; the exact number would be the MILP's SoC shadow price, not persisted), `decisionLabel` (`solarladen|netzladen|entladen|ruhe`, the server twin of `schedule.ts chargeKind`, same 0.05 kW deadband) and a composed German `whyText`. Null wherever honestly not computable (no persisted spot, pre-P2 rows, battery-less site) - never a fake 0. The §14a limit a run used is NOT persisted, so it is not returned (known gap); forecast-model lifecycle comes from `forecast-quality`.
- **Pricing consistency is the load-bearing invariant:** `optimizer/SlotEconomics.java` mirrors `services/optimization/voltpilot_optimization/pricing.py` rule-for-rule (tarif_art branching; DV Marktprämie `max(aw − Monatsmarktwert, 0)` suspended at negative spot + missing-month = no premium; merchant mode = bare spot despite configured EEG remuneration; eigenverbrauch feste Vergütung incl. §51a and 20-year expiry), and `optimizer/EegRates.java` is the Java twin of `config.py`'s `DEFAULT_EEG_RATE_SCHEDULE` + tranche blending - `EegRatesTest` pins the SAME vectors as `test_pricing.py`, so a schedule change must land on both sides. The api reads the same `OPTIMIZER_EEG_RATES_JSON` env.
- **`GET`/`PUT .../optimizer-config`**: platform defaults vs. per-site/per-asset overrides vs. effective values. Overrides (NULL = platform default, additive nullable columns): `asset.wear_cost_ct_per_kwh` (V20260710000000), `asset.soc_min_pct`/`soc_max_pct` (**V20260710020000**, the formerly hard-coded 5-95% band from `domain.py` - now per-battery-asset, read by `inputs.load_battery_sites` with an inconsistent-band fall-back-and-warn), `site.backup_reserve_soc_pct` (V20260710010000). The PUT is FULL-REPRESENTATION (null clears; battery fields without a battery asset = 409; effective min ≥ max = 400). The pre-existing site levers (netzladen/plant_kind/tariff/anzulegender Wert) are echoed read-only - they stay on `PUT /api/v1/sites/{id}`. Terminal-value config is read-only (env-level).
- **The api mirrors the optimizer's env** via `voltpilot.optimizer.*` (`OptimizerProperties`): BOTH composes pass `OPTIMIZER_WEAR_COST_CT_PER_KWH` / `OPTIMIZER_TERMINAL_VALUE_*` / `OPTIMIZER_EEG_RATES_JSON` to the api service too - keep api + optimization env in sync (the `VOLTPILOT_ACTIVE_*_MODEL` discipline) or the panel displays defaults the solver is not using.
- **The controller carries a `ResponseStatusException` `@ExceptionHandler`** (the MastrController/AdminSimulation pattern), so every refusal on this surface - including the pre-existing config 400/409 - arrives as a German `{message}` body the panel can render instead of a bare status.
- **Tests:** `EegRatesTest` + `SlotEconomicsTest` (pure unit, always run - pricing vectors, wear derivation, stored-value math, decision label, whyText, null discipline); `AdminApiTest.optimizerDiagnosticsDecomposeSlotsWithRealTariffAndRemuneration` (hand-computed DV dynamic-tariff + Marktprämie/§51 and EV fest-tariff + feste-Vergütung slots, run selection, auth/tenancy incl. wrong-tenant 404) + `optimizerConfigMergesDefaultsWithOverridesAndWritesThem` (defaults/override/effective merge, writes land on the exact columns `inputs.py` reads, clear-to-default, band validation, battery-less 409, auth); optimization `test_freshness.py` SoC-band section (NULL = defaults, configured band, inconsistent-band fallback).

## What-if re-optimize (`POST /api/v1/admin/sites/{siteId}/optimizer-what-if`, design §4.3)

The last piece of the admin optimizer surface: an operator moves a knob and gets a freshly solved plan to compare, **without touching the plant**. Three properties carry it, and they are the whole design.

- **Ephemeral by construction, not by convention.** `services/optimization/voltpilot_optimization/whatif.py` imports NEITHER `persistence` NOR either publisher - it only calls the pure `gather_inputs` → `optimize` pair, so no route on any side can make it write a `schedule` row or push a retained MQTT plan. `test_whatif.py` pins that at the IMPORT GRAPH (an `ast` walk over the module), which is the check that survives a future edit; the 15-min tick loop runs in a different process and shares no state with it.
- **Two solves over ONE gathered input.** A knob delta is only meaningful on the same prices/forecasts/SoC, so every run gathers once and solves twice: `baseline` (the site as configured) and `variant` (with the overrides). Comparing the variant against the site's PERSISTED run was deliberately rejected - that run was generated at another time over another horizon with another price vintage, so the delta would attribute the passage of time to the knob. The persisted run stays what is in force and the portal labels it so.
- **Overrides are the panel's knobs only** (§2.8/§4.3): wear cost, backup-reserve floor, the usable SoC band, and the `netzladen_erlaubt` posture. Prices, forecasts, tariff, §14a and the feed-in cap come from the real site, so a preview can never be a fantasy plant. Every field is OPTIONAL and ABSENT means "leave it as the site has it" - never zero, which is what makes an empty body the meaningful baseline. `backupReserveSocPct: 0` IS "no reserve" (a 0 % floor and no floor are the same constraint), so there is no separate clear flag.

Facts worth knowing before touching it:

- **It rides the EXISTING solve service, not a new container.** `POST /what-if` sits next to `/simulations` on the optimization image's `simulate-serve` surface (`simulation/server.py`, whose docstring now calls it the on-demand solve surface), so the api reuses `voltpilot.simulation.base-url` + the `SimulationHttp` seam - no compose change, no second base-url to keep in sync across dev and prod. The two POST routes are the two shapes of "solve something the tick loop did not ask for": the Ersparnis-Simulation is a long ASYNC year chain behind a job store, a what-if is a single horizon that solves in well under a second and is therefore **synchronous, in the request thread, with no job id**. Concurrency is bounded by a semaphore (`WHATIF_MAX_CONCURRENT`, default 2, → 429) so an admin leaning on the button cannot starve the simulation jobs sharing the container's CPU budget.
- **The api's RLS check IS the tenancy fence.** The solve service resolves sites with the trusted backend credentials and has no tenant concept, so `AdminOptimizerController.requireSite` must prove the site is visible in the switched tenant BEFORE the id is forwarded; a foreign site is 404 and never reaches the service (pinned by `AdminWhatIfApiTest.aForeignSiteIs404AndTheSolveServiceIsNeverContacted`, which also asserts the same site IS reachable through its own tenant so the 404 is the fence and not a broken route).
- **`load_battery_sites(dsn, site_id=None)`** grew the optional filter so a what-if resolves the site through the SAME path the tick loop uses - a preview can never disagree with the plan that will actually run. `terminal_value_eur_per_kwh` is deliberately NOT carried across: `gather_inputs` leaves it at the env override (normally None), so `effective_terminal_value_eur_per_kwh` re-derives V_end from the VARIANT's own posture (flipping netzladen changes which slots may refill the battery).
- **Portal (`pages/admin/OptimizerPage` `WhatIfPanel` + `WhatIfCompareChart`).** The knob form, a Speicherschonung preset row that only PREFILLS the wear field (sharing the 1/4/8 ct ladder with the customer-facing `web/Speicherschonung`), the delta table and the two-plans-in-one chart. ALL derivation/copy is the pure `src/optimizer.ts` (`buildWhatIfRequest`/`whatIfDeltaRows`/`whatIfVerdict`/`overrideChips`/`whatIfFallbackNote`, tested in `optimizerWhatIf.test.ts`). Four honesty rules are load-bearing: an untouched field is ABSENT from the request (else the baseline stops being "as configured"); the "Simulation - es wird nichts an die Anlage gesendet" banner is always visible, not a toast; a delta row is toned only where direction IS a value judgement (more savings good, more wear bad, throughput/cycles neutral); and a failed run DROPS the previous result rather than letting stale numbers answer the new question. `whatIfFallbackNote` says so when only one of the two runs had to drop the §14a cap - the numbers are then not comparable.
- **The comparison chart is its own component but not its own stack:** it reuses `useEChart`/`chartTheme`/`chargeKind`/`ChartLegend` exactly like `OptimizerPlanChart` (variant solid, baseline a pale outline behind it), because "what did the run in force do and why" and "what does this knob change" are different questions over different data.
- **Tests:** `test_whatif.py` (38 cases: the import-graph guard, each knob moves only its own field, the runner gathers once, request validation, and HiGHS-backed proofs that empty overrides yield two identical plans, that a high wear rate stops the cycling, that forbidding grid charging removes the arbitrage and that a reserve floor holds at any price); `AdminWhatIfApiTest` (6 Testcontainers cases with a fake in-process solve service: only-touched-knobs forwarded, empty body = as configured, the RLS fence, customer 403 / anonymous 401, battery-less 409 + invalid knobs refused before any solve, and every upstream refusal keeping its German reason while an outage never looks like a result); portal `optimizerWhatIf.test.ts` + the `OptimizerPage.test.tsx` what-if block.

## Peak Shaving / Lastspitzenkappung (PS-1 + PS-2, cloud side)

Captain go 2026-07-16 (spec: scout `vp-battery-models-b9` report Teil 3): RLM sites pay a **Leistungspreis** (€/kW per billing period) on the period's highest 15-min mean grid IMPORT; the battery shaves it. Cloud planning, the edge peak guard (PS-3) and the customer-facing reporting (PS-4) are all BUILT - see the last two bullets.

- **Master data (api migration `V20260716020000`):** nullable `site.leistungspreis_eur_kw` (**non-NULL IS the module flag** - the max_feed_in philosophy, no separate boolean), `site.abrechnung_leistung` (`jahr`|`monat`, NOT NULL default `jahr`, Europe/Berlin calendar periods), nullable `site.peak_reserve_soc_pct` (PS-2), plus `schedule.peak_target_kw` (run-level fact repeated per slot, the terminal_value pattern; mirrored in `infra/local/timescale/04-schedule.sql`). **Configuration is ADMIN-ONLY** (captain: VoltPilot richtet vertragsnahe Module ein): the three fields are written EXCLUSIVELY via the platform-admin `PUT .../optimizer-config` (`peakShaving` block, full-representation - null LP = module off, null abrechnung resets to `jahr`) and echoed read-only on `SiteDto`; they deliberately do NOT exist on Create/UpdateSiteRequest.
- **`peak_so_far` is computed FRESH per optimizer cycle, never stored** (`inputs._peak_so_far_kw`, module-active sites only): `max(grid_import_kwh)*4.0` from `telemetry_rollup_15m` since the Berlin period start (`billing_period_start`), blended (max) with the RUNNING quarter-hour's raw `avg(greatest(power_kw,0))` because the rollup refresh lags ≤15 min. **Plausibility gate** (`plausible_peak`, `config.PEAK_SPIKE_FACTOR` 3.0): the period's top bucket is discarded for the second-highest when it exceeds 3× it - one unfiltered legacy-edge telemetry spike must not anchor the whole year; a real recurring peak (similar top buckets) always survives.
- **MILP (`solver.py`, in BOTH builds - economic, no new infeasibility path, §14a fallback untouched):** epigraph `peak >= grid_import_t`, `peak >= peak_so_far`, objective `+ LP*(peak − peak_so_far)` (exact for the period; no dt - it is a per-POWER price) `+ peak_ratchet_eur_per_kw(LP) * peak_below` where `peak_below` is the plain horizon peak. **The ratchet weight is `min(3%·LP, 0.30 €/kW)`** - the cap is load-bearing and was proven necessary: uncapped 3% of a Jahres-LP (100-200 €/kW → 3-6 €/kW per plan) PROVABLY dominates real arbitrage (a 400 €/MWh spread earns only ~0.6 €/kW of charge power - the solver covered cheap load instead of exporting at 500). The report's binding requirements (wear-scale, "nie echte Arbitrage dominieren") win over its "z. B. 2-5%" example; derivation in `config.py`. Export is NEVER in the epigraph (import-only instrument).
- **PS-2 reserve:** `BatteryParams.peak_reserve_pct` joins the reservation STACK via the same `soc_floor_kwh` machinery as the P11 backup reserve (incl. below-floor-start relaxation): each reservation is an ABSOLUTE SoC floor, `max` binds (technical < backup < peak-reserve) - NOT additive; hard at any price.
- **Publish/persist:** module-active plans carry the ADDITIVE optional top-level contract fields **`grid_import_limit_kw`** (= the solved `peak`, the future edge peak-guard target) and **`peak_reserve_soc_pct`** (`mqtt-schedule.schema.json`, schema_version stays 1.0; absent = no module, old edges ignore both safely - documented x-failsafe); `schedule.peak_target_kw` persists the target per run (NULL = module off, never fabricated).
- **Tests:** optimization `tests/test_peak_shaving.py` (hand-computed: hold-vs-economically-break the peak, ratchet shaves below the anchor after a torn peak / never blocks a profitable cycle, period-start flattening, export unpenalized, reserve stack + hard floor, §14a compose + fallback-build shaving, EEG discharge-only, contract fields, billing-period/gate/wiring units) + `test_freshness.py` column resolution; api `AdminApiTest.peakShavingModuleIsAdminConfiguredAndEchoedReadOnlyOnTheSite` (writes land on the exact columns `inputs.py` reads, SiteDto read-only echo, customer path cannot touch them, validation, authz/tenancy).
- **PS-3 edge peak guard (`edge-app/core`, the local closed loop - the billing peak is a 15-min MEAN, so a stochastic load jump at :07 must be counter-regulated INSIDE the running quarter hour; the 15-min cloud MPC can only plan peaks).** `plan.Parse` reads the two optional contract fields (validated; reserve only alongside a valid target; nil = module off = byte-for-byte pre-PS, persisted with the plan across reboots); the accessors `PeakImportLimit()`/`PeakReserveSoc()` are DELIBERATELY staleness-independent - on a dead cloud link the guard keeps defending the LAST known target (contract x-failsafe amended for PS-3; safe because restrict-only). `guards.PeakTracker` integrates the gated composite `power_kw` (import-only - RLM meters register per direction, export never offsets; hold-last between samples, wall-clock :00/:15/:30/:45 quarters via `Truncate` - Berlin offset is whole hours so no tzdata; >2-min measurement gap = tracker inactive, never regulate blind) and projects the remaining-quarter import allowance; `guards.PeakShave` runs AFTER `Clamp` in `applySetpoint` (BOTH modes) and only ever LOWERS the setpoint toward a non-negative import target, respecting rated discharge + the SoC floor - so §14a (both directions), EEG solar-only and the band are provably never violated. **Fallback composition:** ordinary self-consumption discharge stops at the plan-carried `peak_reserve_soc_pct`; peak DEFENSE may discharge below it down to `SocMinPct` (that is what the reserve is for); a fresh plan's own setpoints are never reserve-floored (the cloud plans its own stack). `/api/state` additively exposes `peak_target_kw`/`peak_reserve_soc_pct`/`peak_guard_active`/`peak_quarter_mean_kw` → the `:8484` "Betrieb" card (`static/betrieb.js`, //go:embed - rebuild core after edits). Tests: `guards/peakguard_test.go` (tracker boundaries/gap/backfill/allowance math, PeakShave incl. compliance-composition proof), `plan` parse/round-trip, `agent/peak_guard_test.go` (reserve floor, defense-below-reserve, legacy byte-for-byte, schedule-mode shave, blind-inactive, telemetry plumbing), `web` (card structure + envelope), e2e-compose publishes the fields.
- **PS-4 reporting ("Vermiedene Spitze: X kW × Y €/kW = Z €", read-only, no schema change):** `GET /api/v1/earnings` carries a per-site **`peakShaving`** block - present exactly when the module is active (non-NULL Leistungspreis), RANGE-INDEPENDENT like `expectedMarketValue*` (always the RUNNING Berlin billing period). `repo/PeakShavingRepository` computes per period from `telemetry_rollup_15m`: measured peak = `max(grid_import_kwh)*4`, counterfactual no-battery peak = `max(greatest(import − export + discharge − charge, 0))*4` (== `max(0, load − pv)` per the power-balance identity - the same plant, battery idle; approximations documented in its Javadoc), `avoidedKw = max(0, baseline − measured)` (floor: a battery-CAUSED peak reads 0, never negative), `avoidedEur = avoidedKw × LP` NOT pro-rated (the standing saving; final once the period closes; current contract price applied to the 12-period `history` too). Buckets without `grid_import_kwh` are skipped; a module site without measurements gets config echo + null peaks (never fabricated). Portal: the Optimierung card's proof rows (`lastspitzenProof` in `moduleSurface.ts`, InfoTip "ohne Speichereinsatz" on the Vermiedene-Spitze row; `OptimierungSection` fetches `api.earnings('month')` fail-soft) + ONE calm `vermiedeneSpitzeLine` in the `AnlageHero` money hero (hidden below the 0.05 kW deadband). A Peak-Verlauf chart (measured vs. `schedule.peak_target_kw` per period) is deliberately NOT built yet - the `history` data for it is already in the response. Proven by `PortalApiTest.earningsExposeThePeakShavingProofPerBillingPeriod` + portal `moduleSurface.test.ts`/`OptimierungSection.test.tsx`.

## Historie (history/reporting: `services/api` history package + portal "Historie" page)

The customer-facing history view (captain's decisions: **money lens leads**, private customers first, core value **Nachvollziehbarkeit** - the customer can see and verify what the system did and why).
Read-only feature: one migration, one endpoint, one portal page.

- **Telemetry rollups instead of continuous aggregates.** TimescaleDB **hard-refuses caggs on hypertables with row-level security** ("cannot create continuous aggregate on hypertable with row security", verified on 2.17.2), and `telemetry` has FORCE RLS.
  So api migration **`V20260701030000`** builds the same outcome from primitives that respect the RLS spine: plain rollup **hypertables** `telemetry_rollup_15m/1h/1d` (per-bucket energies pv/load/grid-import/grid-export/battery-charge/battery-discharge kWh + SoC min/max/last + `n_samples`), RLS-scoped exactly like telemetry, refreshed by a TimescaleDB **background job** (`add_job`, 15-min interval) running an idempotent upsert cascade (raw -> 15m -> 1h -> 1d) over a 7-day trailing window as the job owner = the Flyway superuser (bypasses RLS to aggregate all tenants).
  The migration backfills all raw history once; data arriving >7 days late needs a manual `CALL refresh_telemetry_rollups(<since>)`.
  Raw `telemetry` stays untouched (no retention - the raw corpus is future ML training data).
  Semantics live in the migration header: energy = avg(power over the bucket's samples) x bucket hours with per-sample import/export split; actual battery power derived from the power balance (`battery_kw = power_kw - load_kw + pv_power_kw`); daily buckets are **Europe/Berlin** days (the v1 platform timezone, `HistoryRange.ZONE`).
- **Endpoint** `GET /api/v1/sites/{siteId}/history?range=day|week|month|year&at=<date>` (SiteController -> `history/HistoryService` -> RLS-scoped `HistoryRepository`; foreign site 404, bad range 400; in `openapi.yaml`).
  Day = live 15-min buckets straight from raw telemetry (never behind the rollup refresh); week/month/year = the rollups, with per-bucket grid cost merged from the 15m-rollup x `day_ahead_prices` join.
  Totals formulas documented on `HistoryTotalsDto`: grid cost = 15-min import energy x matching day-ahead price; battery savings = sum(baseline - cost) over persisted `schedule` slots taking the **latest run per slot** (`DISTINCT ON (time) ... ORDER BY generated_at DESC`, so overlapping MPC runs never double-count; **null** where no plan exists); Autarkiegrad = 1 - import/consumption; Eigenverbrauchsquote = (pv - export)/pv.
  **Totals honesty (audit V2/X1/H3/H8, 2026-07-24):** the four ENERGY sums follow the cost fields' discipline - **null, never 0**, when not a single bucket carried that channel (a 0-bucket day used to return a confident `pvGenerationKwh: 0.0` next to the board's honest "noch keine Daten"), and a ratio whose numerator or denominator is unknown stays null. The ex-ante plan sum is served as **`batterySavingsPlannedEur`** (`batterySavingsEur` survives ONE release as a same-valued deprecated alias) because it is NOT measured money - the measured counterpart is `savedEur` on `/api/v1/earnings` and the two legitimately differ ~8x on the same plant/period, so any surface rendering it must say "geplant". `gridCostEur` is the REAL supply cost since Stufe 3 of the structured Bezugspreis: import energy × the same `SlotEconomics.importPriceCtSql` composition the optimizer plans with (bare spot only without any price data - byte-identical to before); the additive **`tarifArt`** + **`tarifPriced`** label it honestly (`tarifPriced=false` = the number must read "zu Börsenpreisen", true = "bewertet zu Ihrem Stromtarif"; a flat tariff prices even unpriced buckets).
  Day range additionally returns the **Tagesprotokoll** (`history/Tagesprotokoll`, pure functions: battery charge/discharge windows above a 0.1 kW noise threshold with energy-weighted avg price and avoided-cost EUR, PV peak, price extremes; plain-German server-formatted texts) and the **plan-vs-actual** trajectory (latest persisted plan value per 15-min slot).
- **Bucket -> Preis geht NUR über `repo/PriceSlots`, nie wieder über ein LATERAL pro Bucket (gemessener Defekt, Scout `vp-historie-konzept-t4` §5, behoben 2026-07-30).** Die alte Zuordnung `LEFT JOIN LATERAL (... ORDER BY (p.resolution = 'PT15M') DESC, p.ts DESC LIMIT 1)` sortiert nach einem **Ausdruck**, also kann Postgres `idx_day_ahead_prices_zone_ts` nicht zum vorzeitigen Abbruch nutzen: das `LIMIT 1` greift erst nach dem Sortieren, jeder Bucket las und verwarf ~1.400 Preiszeilen (`loops=3625`, 111.985 Buffer-Hits, **1,2 s** für ein Jahresfenster - auf dem Demo-Stack reproduziert), und dieselbe Form lief **fünfmal pro `/earnings`-Anfrage**. `PriceSlots` baut die Preisreihe **einmal** je (Zone, Fenster) als `MATERIALIZED`-CTE `price_slot(bidding_zone, slot, price_eur_mwh)` - PT60M auf Viertelstunden aufgefaltet, Gewinner je Slot per `DISTINCT ON` mit **denselben** Sortierschlüsseln - danach ist es ein Equi-Join: **~9-18 ms**, und die Kosten wachsen nicht mehr mit der Zahl der Buckets. Drei Dinge, die man dabei kennen muss: (1) die einzige Annahme ist ein **viertelstunden-ausgerichtetes Join-Raster** (Rollup-Buckets, `time_bucket`, `forecast.time` erfüllen sie; die Preiszeilen selbst dürfen beliebig liegen - ihr erster abgedeckter Slot wird abgeleitet); (2) die Auffaltung nutzt bewusst ein 4-zeiliges `VALUES` statt `generate_series` - eine SRF im LATERAL kostet ~30 µs Executor-Setup **pro Preiszeile** und misst 100 ms, wo das `VALUES` 3 ms misst; (3) das Fenster-Prädikat rechnet auf einem Bind-Parameter und braucht deshalb `?::timestamptz`, sonst hält Postgres den Platzhalter für ein `interval`. Semantik-Abnahme ist `PriceSlotEqualityTest` (Testcontainers): das alte LATERAL steht dort wörtlich als Orakel und wird Slot für Slot gegen `PriceSlots` verglichen - nur PT15M, nur PT60M, gemischt, Lücken, unausgerichtete `ts`, unbekannte Auflösung, `ts DESC`-Gleichstand, und eine Preiszeile die VOR dem Fenster beginnt (der Wächter für die 75-Minuten-Untergrenze).
- **Portal surface**: **TWO worlds, two routes** (captain structure H1, 2026-07-30; concept `data/vp-historie-konzept-t4`) — `#/anlage/{id}/messwerte` (`pages/MesswerteSection.tsx`, BASE: the energy sums + the one multi-series chart carrying PV/Haus/Netz/Batterie/Ladestand + the Messwerte-Explorer as a disclosure) and `#/anlage/{id}/erloese` (`pages/ErloeseSection.tsx`, MODE-BOUND on `surface.ts` `erloes-historie`: Stromkosten, the GEPLANTE Speicher-Ersparnis, the Speicher-&-Preis proof, Tagesprotokoll). `#/anlage/{id}/historie` redirects to `messwerte` **preserving `?m=`/`z=`/`at=`**. Both share ONE skeleton (world head with an honesty badge + one-click world switch that carries the period, sticky time bar, cards, foot card) and ONE fetch+cache (`historyCache.ts`). Seit PR D trägt die Zeit-Leiste zusätzlich den **Zeitraum-Sprung** (natives Datums-/Wochen-/Monatsfeld + Jahresauswahl + der wiederverwendete `MonthStrip` ohne erfundene Werte), das **Δ zur Vorperiode** (ein zweiter Abruf desselben Endpunkts durch denselben Cache — nie gegen eine erfundene Null, gewertet nur wo die Richtung eindeutig ist) und die **Datenabdeckung**. Derivation rules, honesty badges (Gemessen · Bewertet · Geplant) and the measured CSS/ECharts footguns: `frontend/portal/AGENTS.md` "Die Historie sind ZWEI WELTEN".
- **Datenabdeckung des Zeitraums (F4/P7, additiv auf der `/history`-Antwort):** `HistoryDto.coverage` (`HistoryCoverageDto`) beantwortet, was die Seite bisher verschwieg — ab wann es überhaupt Daten gibt und wie viel des Zeitraums wirklich gemessen ist. Es ist eine **EIGENE billige Abfrage** (`HistoryRepository.coverage`: `min/max/count` + `lag()` auf `telemetry_rollup_15m`, RLS-gefenced wie alles hier), bewusst NICHT aus dem Datenblock abgeleitet — sie bleibt damit unabhängig von der Reihe UND von der Preisreihe (`PriceSlots`), berührt deren Equi-Join also nicht. Gezählt werden **Viertelstunden** (`resolutionMinutes`), nicht Anzeige-Buckets: so entsteht die Messreihe, egal ob die Seite Tage oder Stunden zeichnet.
  Die Arithmetik ist die reine, immer laufende `HistoryService.coverage(row, window, now)`: **erwartet wird erst ab der ersten je gemessenen Viertelstunde** (ein Kalenderjahr einer im Juni ans Netz gegangenen Anlage ist nicht zu 45 % lückenhaft — es gab sie vorher nicht; genau dafür reist `firstDataAt` mit), das Ende liegt eine Rollup-Kadenz (15 min) **vor jetzt** (eine gerade beendete Viertelstunde ist keine Lücke, sondern noch nicht verdichtet), `measured` wird auf `expected` gedeckelt (nie über 100 %), und `gaps` zählt innere PLUS Rand-Fehlstellen. **Kein Prozentsatz im Vertrag** — die Zähler sind die Tatsache, die Formulierung (inkl. „nie auf 100 % aufrunden") gehört der Oberfläche. `null`, wenn die Anlage nie eine Viertelstunde gemessen hat; dokumentierte Grenze: gezählt wird die v1-Messreihe, eine rein v2-native Anlage liefert deshalb keine Abdeckung statt einer behaupteten (eine migrierte ist nicht betroffen — der Fan-out des Writers speist beide Reihen).
- **Die Ereignis-Spur des Verlaufs (F6, additives `HistoryDto.events`) — was den Ausreißer ERKLÄRT, in JEDEM Zeitraum.** Das `Tagesprotokoll` bleibt unverändert die Liste der GEWÖHNLICHEN Ereignisse eines Tages; die neue Spur (`history/Ereignisse.java`, rein wie `Tagesprotokoll`) trägt ausschließlich das Auffällige: `negativpreis` (Preisreihe unter null), `abregelung` (GEPLANT aus `schedule.curtail_kw` — der Text sagt „eingeplant"), `netzgrenze` (§ 14a aus `telemetry.grid_limit_kw`), `netzladen` (gemessen), `datenluecke` und `geraet-still`. Der Vertrag ist bewusst schmal (`HistoryEventDto`: type/start/end/text): **`text` ist die Aussage OHNE Zeitangabe** — die Zeit steht in `start`/`end` und wird von der Oberfläche zeitraumgerecht davorgesetzt. Fenster entstehen durch Verschmelzen aufeinanderfolgender Viertelstunden; je Art werden höchstens `MAX_PER_TYPE` (20) ausgeliefert, die LÄNGSTEN zuerst.
  Vier schmale, additive Abfragen liefern je Viertelstunde EINE Tatsache (`HistoryRepository.negativePriceSlots`/`curtailSlots`/`gridLimitSlots`/`gridChargeSlots` + `dataGaps`, alle RLS-gefenced wie der Rest); Fenster, Texte und Obergrenzen rechnet die reine Klasse — dasselbe Muster wie `CoverageRow`. Drei Entscheidungen, die man kennen muss:
  - **§ 14a wird nur für Tag und Woche ausgewertet** (`Ereignisse.evaluatesGridLimit`): `grid_limit_kw` steht NUR in der rohen `telemetry`, die Rollup-Kaskade führt die Spalte nicht — ein Jahresfenster wäre ein Scan über Millionen Rohzeilen. **Die Oberfläche muss das aussprechen**, sonst läse sich das Fehlen eines Markers als „keine Netzgrenze"; der Zwilling ist `frontend/portal/src/historieEreignisse.ts` `spurHinweis` — beide zusammen ändern.
  - **Netzladen wird nur behauptet, wo es bewiesen ist:** die Viertelstunde muss PV UND Ladung UND Netzbezug gemessen haben (`pv_kwh IS NOT NULL`), sonst wäre jede Ladung einer PV-losen Messreihe „aus dem Netz". Der genannte Wert ist die vorsichtigere der beiden Schranken (`least(charge − pv, import)`).
  - **Die Lücken-Marker stimmen mit der Zeit-Leiste überein:** `HistoryService` holt die Abdeckungs-Rohzahlen EINMAL und liest sie zweimal — die Zeit-Leiste zählt daraus die Fehlstellen, die Spur setzt ihre Rand-Marker auf dieselben Grenzen. Ein Schluss-Rand ist `geraet-still` statt `datenluecke`, wenn die Anlage auch NACH diesem Zeitraum nichts mehr gemessen hat (`lastInWindow == siteLast`) — der Unterschied zwischen „damals fehlte etwas" und „es kommt gerade nichts".
- **Tests**: `TagesprotokollTest` + `HistoryRangeTest` + **`HistoryCoverageTest`** + **`EreignisseTest`** (pure unit, always run: heuristics on synthetic days, Berlin/DST window arithmetic, totals formulas + null semantics, die Abdeckungs-Rechnung inkl. „erst ab der ersten Messung", laufender Viertelstunde, Rand-Lücken, Deckelung — und die Spur: Fenster-Verschmelzung, die vier Texte, Innen-/Rand-Lücken vs. stilles Gerät, „ohne Abdeckung keine Lücke, Markt-Ereignisse bleiben", Obergrenze + Chronologie); `PortalApiTest.historyDayComputesTotalsProtocolAndPlanOverlay` + `historyWeekAggregatesFromTheRollupTables` + **`historyCarriesTheDataCoverageOfThePeriod`** (real TimescaleDB + Keycloak: fixed past-day seed with hand-computed expectations, protocol events, plan overlay, the rollup path incl. `CALL refresh_telemetry_rollups`, RLS 404, bad range 400).

## Shadow-mode forecasting (model registry + Prognosequalität)

The captain-approved ML path with **Nachvollziehbarkeit as the core requirement** (full lifecycle doc: [`docs/forecasting.md`](docs/forecasting.md)).
The decision layer stays the exact MILP; the forecast layer is a measurable, comparable, swappable **model registry**: an ML challenger must PROVABLY beat the baseline on recorded history before it may ever influence a plan - and the evidence is visible in the portal in plain German.

- **Registry + tagging** (`voltpilot_forecast/registry.py`): every forecaster carries a stable model id; every persisted `forecast` row carries it in the `model` column (PK widened to include it). Baselines `load-persistence` (`SeasonalPersistenceLoadForecaster`) and `pv-physical` (`PhysicalPvForecaster`) stay the ACTIVE defaults. Challengers `load-xgb` and `pv-residual-xgb` run in **shadow**: they predict + persist every cycle, nothing downstream consumes them.
- **Active model = DATA since the promotion switch (Captain 18.08.2026); the env is its default.** See the "Prognose-Beförderung" section below for the full contract. The three consumers - the **optimizer** (`inputs.py` filters its forecast reads on the active model, `tests/test_active_model.py`), the **forecast collector/evaluator** and the **api** (the "live" badge) - all resolve the SAME precedence (portal choice > `VOLTPILOT_ACTIVE_*_MODEL` > registry baseline), so they cannot disagree. There is still NO automatic promotion.
- **The challengers** (`voltpilot_forecast/ml.py`, optional `[ml]` extra = xgboost+numpy via the native Booster API - no sklearn; LightGBM's wheels were no friendlier, both need OpenMP: macOS `brew install libomp`, the Dockerfile installs libgomp1): `load-xgb` = XGBoost on tabular features (`features.py`: Berlin-local slot-of-day/weekday/weekend + German-holiday flag (`holidays.py`, Gauss Easter), leakage-safe lags -1d/-7d, windowed means, outdoor temperature from stored weather); `pv-residual-xgb` = the physical PV model + a learned residual on weather features (prediction = physical + residual, clipped to [0, capacity]). Deterministic seeds.
- **Self-gating (honesty)**: a challenger refuses to train below **21 full telemetry days** (`VOLTPILOT_ML_MIN_DAYS`; a day counts at >= 48 of 96 slots) - below the gate it emits NO predictions and records `status='collecting'` + days_collected/required in `forecast_model_state`, which the portal renders as "Sammelt Daten: Tag X von 21". Nightly retrain (first collector cycle per Berlin day) persists the explainability trail: trained_at, train_rows, top-5 feature importances with plain-German labels.
- **Collector** (`forecast_collect.py`, console script `voltpilot-forecast-collect`, compose `feeds` profile as **`forecast-collector`**, prod service `forecast`): every 15 min per site it loads telemetry + stored weather (latest run per hour - past hours = "the weather that was forecast then"), runs baselines + gated challengers, persists all model-tagged, upserts `forecast_model_state`, and once per Berlin day triggers the evaluation - one container for collect + retrain + evaluate. PV nameplate is estimated from the observed generation peak (+5 %) until plants are modelled.
- **Evaluation** (`evaluation.py` pure math + `evaluate.py` DB glue, `voltpilot-forecast-eval`): per site x model x **Berlin day**, compares each model's per-slot FRESHEST prediction issued at/before the slot (`run_at <= time` - what the optimizer would have consumed) against 15-min telemetry actuals -> `forecast_accuracy` (MAE kW, nMAE % - NULL on all-zero days, bias, **skill = 1 - mae/mae_reference where the reference is the kind's ACTIVE model**, NULL for the reference itself). Plan economics -> `plan_accuracy` (freshest ex-ante plan cost + no-battery baseline vs realized signed grid cost from telemetry x price, over exactly the comparable slots). All upserts idempotent - re-running a day after late telemetry overwrites.
- **Schema** (api migration **`V20260701040000`**, canonical runtime owner): retrofits/creates `forecast.model` (backfills old rows as the baseline ids, widens the PK) and owns `forecast_model_state`, `forecast_accuracy`, `plan_accuracy` - all three tenant-stamped + RLS-scoped exactly like telemetry (writers = trusted backend role, api reads via the app role). `forecast` itself stays without RLS (backend-only consumers). Service-side mirror migration `services/forecast/migrations/V20260702000000__forecast_model_column.sql`; bootstrap mirrors in `infra/local/timescale/02-forecast.sql` + `infra/prod/timescale/00-collector-tables.sql`.
- **API + portal**: `GET /api/v1/sites/{siteId}/forecast-quality` (SiteController; active models RESOLVED by `ForecastModelService` - not raw config -, model states, daily error/skill series, plan economics; foreign site 404, fresh site = empty well-formed lists; in `openapi.yaml`) feeds the portal page **"Prognosequalität"** (`pages/PrognosePage.tsx`, nav id `prognose`): live model per Verbrauch/PV with Ø-Abweichung (7 Tage), challenger cards (honest "Sammelt Daten: Tag X von 21" progress vs "In X der letzten Y Bewertungen genauer"), per-kind MAE comparison chart (`ForecastQualityChart.tsx`: active solid blue, challenger dashed orange), top-feature list in plain German, and the shadow-mode explainer ("keinerlei Einfluss ... automatisch passiert das nie").
- **Tests (the assurance)**: forecast - `test_registry.py` (tagging + env resolution + per-model repository isolation), `test_holidays.py`/`test_features.py` (computus; leakage-free lags; every feature has a German label), `test_ml.py` (gating below/at threshold, deterministic seed, **skill sanity: the learner beats persistence on a weekly pattern with skill > 0.5** and the PV residual corrects a systematic bias), `test_evaluation.py` (exact hand-computed MAE/nMAE/skill/plan-economics, DST day bounds, idempotent upserts), `test_collect.py` (full collector cycle over a fake DB + evaluate_day end to end); optimization - `test_active_model.py` (only active-model rows consumed; env flip switches). Api: `PortalApiTest.forecastQualityIsTenantScopedAndMarksTheActiveModel` (real TimescaleDB+Keycloak, RLS 404). CI runs the forecast matrix with `.[dev,ml]` so the challenger tests run in the gate.

## Prognose-Beförderung: der Schalter im Portal, JE ANLAGE (`site_forecast_model_choice`)

Captain-Auftrag 18.08.2026 („Kannst du einen Schalter im Portal dafür machen und den Schattenbetrieb für den Benutzer erklärbarer machen?"), **revidiert am 19.08.2026: die Wahl gilt PRO ANLAGE, und JEDER KUNDE trifft sie für seine eigenen Anlagen selbst.** Die Messung des Schattenbetriebs lief seit Langem; die daraus folgende HANDLUNG war eine Umgebungsvariable an drei Containern plus Redeploy, dann ein plattformweiter Admin-Schalter — beides fachlich zu grob: welches Modell am besten passt, hängt an der EINZELNEN Anlage (Lastprofil, Wetterlage, Speichergröße), und die Entscheidung über die eigene Anlage gehört dem Kunden. Betreiber-Anleitung: [`docs/forecasting.md`](docs/forecasting.md) „Promotion (and rollback)".

- **⚠ DIE PRÄZEDENZ IST DER GANZE VERTRAG und steht WORTGLEICH an vier Stellen** (die zwei Migrationen `V20260825000000`/`V20260826000000`, `ForecastModels.resolve` (api), `voltpilot_forecast/model_choice.py` + `ModelChoices.for_site`, `voltpilot_optimization/inputs.active_model`): **die jüngste Zeile in `site_forecast_model_choice` je (Anlage, Art) gewinnt → sonst die jüngste Zeile in `forecast_model_choice` je Art (die PLATTFORM-Vorgabe) → sonst die Umgebungsvariable → sonst der Registry-Default (das Basismodell)**. **Ohne eine einzige Zeile ist jeder Pfad byte-identisch zu vorher** (gepinnt von `test_active_model.py` + `ForecastModelsTest`). Umgekehrt: auf einer umgestellten Anlage ist ein späterer Env-Edit WIRKUNGSLOS, und auch ein Betreiber-Klick auf die Plattform-Vorgabe nimmt ihr die Entscheidung nicht ab. Wer die vier auseinanderlaufen lässt, erzeugt einen Optimierer, der eine andere Reihe liest als die, die das Portal „live" nennt.
- **BEIDE Tabellen SIND ihr Audit-Journal** (append-only, `id BIGSERIAL`, je Zeile `model_kind`/`model_id`/`previous_model_id`/`set_by` (JWT-Subject)/`set_by_name` (preferred_username, Anzeige)/`set_at`; „was gilt" = `DISTINCT ON (…) … ORDER BY …, id DESC`, **nie auf `set_at`** - zwei Umstellungen in derselben Mikrosekunde wären dort ein Gleichstand).
